  reusable assets (`town_pack`: town, home and bar backgrounds, bar ambience —
  the main game uses the same pack) and the engine mods it needs (`shell2d_base`,
  `dialog_base`, `audio_base`). See "Start your own game" below.
- `collections/cthangover/` — the main game, a visual novel: story, the 2D
  engine, characters, quests, items, battles with an optional second battle
  core, Live2D and tools, each as its own mechanic.
- `collections/rpg_plus/` — an RPG Maker style game on the same engine mods:
  its story and world (`rpg_plus_game`: prologue, village, forest, shrine, desert),
  reusable art (`village_pack`),
  classic rules as data (`classic_rpg_pack`), the stats mods (`stats_base`,
  `stat_checks`, `equipment`, `battle_formulas`), the FF battle core with the
  card core as an option, and as optional tools the map editor (`rpg_editor`)
  and the Data Editor with the item and character editors (`schema_tools`,
  `items_editor`, `characters_editor`).
- `collections/dev_tools/` — the developer toolset to add to any game: the
  individually selectable editors (shared components in `tools_base`; legacy package `editor_tools`), the in-game scenario studio (`scenario_studio`,
  opened by the **Studio** button on the game's toolbar), validators and the
  mod manager. Add it to a game in the collection editor and enter the game
  again — the mod set switches without a restart.

- `collections/junior_reaper_paper_grief/` — a separate Russian-first investigation: Bureau offices, isometric city gates/market/mill yard, mouse-driven mill and home interiors, clue inventory, quest stages and ambient NPC conversations. It uses map-owned events and `npc_barks_base`, and includes the authoring editors and studio. Entry scene: `jrp_arrival`; projection: `isometric`. Full story translations are not implemented yet.

===== DOC docs/mods/configs/game_config.md: Game Config — game_config.json =====
Default game configuration loaded from `config/game_config.json` at startup. Defines audio, display, language, and logging settings.

## File location

```filestree
config/
└── game_config.json
```

## Full schema

```jsonc
{
  // Audio defaults
  "audio": {
    "sounds_enabled": true,       // Sound effects master toggle
    "musics_enabled": true,       // Music master toggle
    "ambient_enabled": true,      // Ambient sounds master toggle
    "sounds_volume": 100,         // SFX volume (0–100)
    "musics_volume": 15,          // Music volume (0–100)
    "ambient_volume": 100         // Ambient volume (0–100)
  },
  // Locale code (UI language)
  "language": "en-en",
  // Display settings
  "display": {
    "window_mode": 3,             // 0=Windowed, 1=Min, 2=Max, 3=Fullscreen, 4=Exclusive
    "resolution_width": 1920,     // Width in pixels
    "resolution_height": 1024,    // Height in pixels
    "current_screen": 0,          // Monitor index (0-based)
    "vsync_enabled": false,       // Vertical sync
    "max_fps": 0                  // Frame cap; 0 is unlimited
  },
  // Logging configuration
  "logging": {
    "enabled": true,              // Master logging on/off
    "console_enabled": true,      // Write logs to console
    "minimum_level": "Debug",     // Min level: Debug, Message, Warning, Error
    "enabled_categories": ["SAVE", "MODS", "..."]
  }
}
```

## `audio` section

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `sounds_enabled` | bool | Master toggle for sound effects |
| `musics_enabled` | bool | Master toggle for music |
| `ambient_enabled` | bool | Master toggle for ambient sounds |
| `sounds_volume` | int | Sound effects volume (0–100) |
| `musics_volume` | int | Music volume (0–100) |
| `ambient_volume` | int | Ambient volume (0–100) |

## `display` section

| Field | Type | Values | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `window_mode` | int | 0=Windowed, 1=Min, 2=Max, 3=Fullscreen, 4=Exclusive | Window display mode (Godot `DisplayServer.WindowMode`) |
| `resolution_width` | int | pixels | Window width; fullscreen uses the monitor resolution |
| `resolution_height` | int | pixels | Window height; fullscreen uses the monitor resolution |
| `current_screen` | int | 0-indexed | Monitor/display index |
| `vsync_enabled` | bool | true/false | Vertical sync |
| `max_fps` | int | 0 or frame cap | 0 means unlimited |

The shipped config uses a 1920×1024 window size; `DisplaySection` code defaults are 1920×1080. The base viewport is 1920×1024 with `canvas_items` stretch. The Display tab exposes window mode, monitor, window size, V-Sync and frame cap; `scale` is not a current field. Windowed sizes are centred on the selected monitor.

## `language` section

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `language` | string | Locale code (e.g. `"en-en"`, `"ru-ru"`) |

## `logging` section

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | bool | Master logging toggle |
| `console_enabled` | bool | Write logs to console output |
| `minimum_level` | string | Minimum log level: `"Debug"`, `"Message"`, `"Warning"`, `"Error"` |
| `enabled_categories` | string[] | Active log channels (see below) |

### Available log categories

Categories are plain strings passed to `GameLogger.Log(category, ...)` — **no `LOG_` prefix**.

| Category | Covers |
|---|---|
| `AUDIO` | Audio playback |
| `BATTLE` | Battle engine events |
| `BATTLE_UI` | Battle interface events |
| `CATALOG` | Remote mod catalog refresh/cache |
| `CATALOG_EXPORT` | Catalog export dialog |
| `CACHE` | Mod cache (shader extraction) |
| `COND` | Condition evaluation |
| `CONFIG` | Configuration loading |
| `CONVERTER` | Scenario converter |
| `DOWNLOADER` | Mod downloads |
| `DUPLICATE` | Duplicate singleton detection warnings |
| `EVENT_BUS` | Mod event bus subscriptions |
| `FACTORY` | Factory loading |
| `GAME` | Game lifecycle |
| `ICON_CACHE` | Catalog icon downloads/cache |
| `GDS_MOD` | GDScript mod loading |
| `INTERACTIVE` | Interactive objects/layers |
| `LANG` | Localization loading |
| `MOD_COLLECTIONS` | Collection store/resolver/plan |
| `MOD_HUB` | Mods & Collections hub screens |
| `MOD_MANAGER` | Mod manager screen actions |
| `MODS` | General mod loading |
| `MODS_ASSEMBLY` | Mod assembly loading |
| `MODS_COMPILE` | Roslyn C# compilation |
| `MODS_EXPORT` | Mod export |
| `MODS_INIT` | Mod initialization |
| `MODS_NATIVE` | Native library handling in mods |
| `MODS_DISCOVERY` | Scanner/loader registry init |
| `MODS_REGISTRY` | Mod registry operations |
| `MODS_SAVE` | Mod save dispatch |
| `MODS_SETTINGS` | Mod settings persistence |
| `MODS_ZIP` | Zip provider file access |
| `QUEST` | Quest system |
| `SAVE` | Save/load operations |
| `SCENE` | Scene transitions |
| `SCENE_CTX` | Scene context nodes |
| `SHADER` | Shader operations |
| `TEST` | Test runner (SceneSwitcher) |
| `TSCN` | `.tscn` scene resolution/parsing |
| `TSCN_TEST` | `.tscn` test driver |
| `UNINSTALLER` | Mod uninstall/cleanup |
| `UI` | UI events |

> **Warning**: the `enabled_categories` whitelist filter is currently **commented out** inside `GameLogger.Log` — category filtering is inert, and all categories are logged regardless of this setting. Note also that the `config/game_config.json` shipped with the game still lists legacy `LOG_`-prefixed strings (e.g. `"LOG_MODS"`); since categories are plain unprefixed strings, those entries never match a real category.

### Log output files

Logs are written to `user://logs/cthangover.log`; entries with level `Error` are also duplicated to `user://logs/cthangover_errors.log`. A `--log-file=<path>` command-line argument overrides the log file location.

## Runtime settings persistence

User-adjusted settings are persisted to `user://settings.json` as indented JSON (`SettingsData.ConfigPath`), separate from this config file. The `game_config.json` only provides defaults — changes made in the settings menu are saved per-user. Note that this is JSON, not an INI `.cfg` file.

===== DOC docs/mods/configs/index.md: Configs — manifest.json =====
Every mod needs a manifest and optionally a properties file. These define the mod's identity, dependencies, C# source compilation rules, and scene lifecycle hooks.

## `manifest.json` — full schema

```jsonc
{
  // Canonical mod identifier (falls back to folder name if omitted)
  "id": "my_mod",
  // Human-readable name shown in the mod menu
  "name": "MyMod",
  // Semver version — used for saves and deduplication
  "version": "1.0.0",
  // Author identifier (arbitrary string)
  "author": "author_name",
  // Short description for the mod menu and catalog
  "description": "What this mod does",
  // Glob patterns for C# files to compile via Roslyn
  "sources": ["src/*.cs", "src/**/*.cs"],
  // Glob patterns for GDScript files (.gd)
  "gd_sources": ["scripts/*.gd"],
  // Catalog category slug or comma-separated combination (e.g. "library, gameplay")
  "category": "library",
  // Glob patterns for native shared libraries (.dll / .so / .dylib)
  "depends_native": ["bin/*.dll"],
  // List of mod IDs that must be loaded before this mod (versioned strings allowed)
  "depends": ["cthangover_game", "ui_base"],
  // Mutually exclusive mod IDs — informational metadata, never enforced (see load-order)
  "conflicts_with": [],
  // Declarative GUI entries anchored to shell slots (panel / overlay)
  "ui": [
    { "id": "my_panel", "kind": "panel", "slot": "ModBeforeUIPanel", "scene_path": "ui/my_panel.tscn" }
  ],
  // Title-screen buttons opening fullscreen mod scenes
  "main_menu": [
    { "id": "my_screen", "scene_path": "menu/my_screen.tscn", "button": { "locale": "mymod/menu_button" } }
  ]
}
```

### Field reference

| Field | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | No | Canonical mod identifier (falls back to folder/zip name) |
| `name` | No | Human-readable mod name (a display value, not the dependency ID) |
| `version` | No | Semver version string (e.g. "1.0.0"). Used by the save system and for deduplication |
| `author` | No | Author identifier |
| `description` | No | Short description |
| `sources` | No | Glob patterns for C# files to compile via Roslyn |
| `test_sources` | No | C# test globs compiled separately in explicit mod-test mode, never into production assemblies |
| `test_suites` | No | Opt-in virtual test mods; each entry has `id`, optional `path` and `activate` prefixes |
| `integrations` | No | Conditional glue: `id`, partner IDs in `with`, optional `path`; see Integrations |
| `fixture` | No | Test-only mod excluded from normal game/all-mod selection; explicitly mount with `--with-mods=` |
| `gd_sources` | No | Glob patterns for `.gd` GDScript files to compile via Godot's engine |
| `depends` | No | List of mod IDs that must be loaded before this mod (versioned strings allowed — see Load Order) |
| `category` | No | Catalog category slug or comma-separated combination (e.g. `"library, gameplay"`). See the catalog page for the full list |
| `depends_native` | No | Glob patterns for native shared libraries (`.dll` / `.so` / `.dylib`). Copied to the mod cache and wired into P/Invoke resolution by `ModNativeLoader` |
| `conflicts_with` | No | Mutually exclusive mod IDs. **Informational only** — parsed and copied into catalog exports, never enforced at runtime (see the load order warning) |
| `ui` | No | Declarative GUI entries anchored to named shell slots — toolbar-toggled `panel`s and code-driven `overlay`s, collected by `UiRegistry` |
| `main_menu` | No | Title-screen buttons; each opens a fullscreen mod `.tscn`, collected by `MenuRegistry` (section `mods`) |

### Sources — glob patterns

- `"src/*.cs"` — all `.cs` files in `src/`, one level deep
- `"src/**/*.cs"` — all `.cs` files recursively

### GDScript sources — `gd_sources`

- `"scripts/*.gd"` — all `.gd` files in `scripts/`, one level deep

GDScript files are compiled by Godot's GDScript engine (no Roslyn needed). A single `.gd` file can implement multiple interfaces simultaneously — actions, conditions, battle executors, item actions, and mod initializers. See GDScript in Mods for the full API reference.

### Dependencies

```jsonc
"depends": ["cthangover_game"]
```

The kernel resolves the selected game or frontend mod set in topological order. `cthangover_game` is ordinary game content and is loaded only when selected or required; it has no privileged first position. C# compilation sees the transitive closure of the mod's declared dependencies. Circular dependencies are invalid.

### Scene lifecycle hooks

There is no `subscriptions` manifest field anymore — it was removed from the schema. To run mod code when a scene loads or unloads:

- declare scene events in a `.scenario` file — see Scenes and Scenarios;
- or annotate a C# class with the `[SceneEvent]` attribute — see Scene Event Metadata.

To attach mod **UI** to a scene or the shell, use the `ui` array (see the field table above and UI Wrappers):

## Real example — Cooking mod

From `mods/cooking/manifest.json`:

```jsonc
{
  "name": "Cooking",
  "author": "ct",
  "description": "Cooking mechanic - full autonomous mod",
  "sources": ["src/*.cs"],
  "ui": [
    // Workbench overlay for the kitchen — toggled by its own code / ui.toggle_panel
    { "id": "cooking_workbench", "kind": "overlay", "scene": "home_kitchen", "slot": "ModLastPanel",
      "scene_path": "scenes/cooking_panel.tscn", "once": true, "show_on_start": false, "priority": 10 },
    // Ration statistics — shown automatically on entering the kitchen
    { "id": "ration_stats", "kind": "overlay", "scene": "home_kitchen", "slot": "Lastground",
      "scene_path": "scenes/ration_statistics_panel.tscn", "once": false, "show_on_start": true, "priority": 20 }
  ]
}
```

Two overlays are anchored to the `home_kitchen` scene: the cooking workbench lives for the whole session (`once`) and shows itself when its code toggles it; the ration statistics panel is created on scene entry (`once: false`) and shown immediately (`show_on_start: true`).

## Real example — Multiple dependencies

From `mods/cooking/manifest.json`:

```jsonc
{
  "id": "cooking",
  // The named dependencies load before this mod
  "depends": [ "cthangover_game>=1", "ui_base>=1", "items_base>=1", "time_base>=1" ]
}
```

## Test suites (`test_suites`)

Integration tests live inside the mod they test. A host manifest lists its suites; each suite folder
(`test_suites/<id>/`) holds an ordinary `manifest.json` and is mounted as the virtual mod `<host>__<id>` only
when a user arg starts with one of its `activate` prefixes (or `--with-test-suites`):

```jsonc
// mods/interactive_base/manifest.json
"test_suites": [ { "id": "interactives", "activate": ["--itest=interactives"] } ]

// mods/interactive_base/test_suites/interactives/manifest.json — depends on the host implicitly
{ "sources": ["src/*.cs"], "depends": ["cthangover_game>=1", "dialog_base>=1"] }
```

A suite may depend on mods its host must not (here the host sits below `cthangover_game`). A normal launch, a
game and `--all-mods` never load a suite. `"fixture": true` marks a whole mod as a test fixture: it is loaded
only when named in `--with-mods=`.

## Creating a new mod — minimal manifest

```jsonc
{
  "name": "MyFirstMod",
  "author": "you",
  "description": "Adds a custom item and a new scenario option"
}
```

Save as `mods/my_first_mod/manifest.json`. The mod is now discoverable. Add it to the game's collection, or launch with `--with-mods=my_first_mod`; normal startup loads the frontend rather than every installed mod. Even without `sources` or `ui`, the mod's scenarios, items, and other data files are discovered automatically from their conventional locations.

===== DOC docs/mods/configs/mod_config.md: Mod Config — mod_config.json =====
Mod system configuration loaded from `config/mod_config.json`. Controls file discovery, caching, and assembly compilation.

## File location

```filestree
config/
└── mod_config.json
```

## Full schema

```jsonc
{
  // Cache compiled assemblies to disk after first compilation (code default: true)
  "use_assembly_cache": true,
  // File/directory patterns excluded from catalog export checksum
  "catalog_ignore_patterns": [".*","bin","obj"],
  // Catalog JSON URLs (multiple are merged)
  "catalog_urls": [],
  // Optional private catalog URL -> bearer token file, never the token itself
  "catalog_token_files": {},
  // Explicit development-time mod unit tests
  "run_mod_tests": false,
  "mod_test_exclude": [],
  // File extensions treated as textures
  "texture_extensions": [".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg"],
  // Audio file extensions (OGG and WAV)
  "audio_extensions": [".ogg", ".wav"],
  // Shader file extensions
  "shader_extensions": [".gdshader", ".gdshaderinclude"],
  // Mod subdirectories scanned for textures
  "texture_groups": ["ui", "backgrounds", "avatars", "icons", "items", "cards", "effects", "skills"],
  // Resource cache configuration
  "cache": {
    // Cache root directory (Godot user:// path)
    "root": "user://mod_cache/",
    // Per-category cache size limits (LRU eviction on overflow)
    "max_sizes": {
      "avatars": 64,
      "backgrounds": 64,
      "items": 64,
      "ui/icons": 64,
      "ui": 64,
      "music": 64,
      "sounds": 64,
      "effects": 64,
      "actions": 128,
      "characters": 128,
      "recipes": 128
    }
  }
}
```

## Section reference

### Top-level fields

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `use_assembly_cache` | bool | Cache compiled mod assemblies to disk (`true` by default in code). Set to `false` to delete cached DLLs and recompile on every launch — useful during mod development |
| `catalog_ignore_patterns` | string[] | File/directory patterns excluded when computing `install_sha256` for catalog export. Default: `[".*"]` excludes all dot-prefixed entries (`.godot/`, `.import/`, `.gitignore`, etc.) |
| `catalog_urls` | string[] | URLs of catalog JSON files to fetch for the online mod browser. Multiple URLs are merged — metadata from highest version wins, `downloads` arrays are combined |
| `catalog_token_files` | object | Exact catalog URL to token-file path; private sources are fetched without redirects |
| `run_mod_tests` | bool | Run mod unit tests (default false); `--run-tests` also enables explicit test mode |
| `mod_test_exclude` | string[] | Mod IDs excluded from that test run |
| `texture_extensions` | string[] | File extensions treated as textures |
| `audio_extensions` | string[] | DTO field; runtime `ModConfig.Load` currently keeps the code-default `.ogg`/`.wav` list instead of copying this parsed field |
| `shader_extensions` | string[] | File extensions treated as shader files |
| `texture_groups` | string[] | Mod subdirectories scanned for textures. A file in `mods/{mod}/ui/image.png` is found because `"ui"` is listed here |
| `cache` | object | Cache configuration (see below) |

### `texture_groups`

Each entry in `texture_groups` corresponds to a mod subdirectory where textures are discovered. The code default is `["ui", "backgrounds", "avatars", "icons", "items", "characters", "effects", "skills"]`; the shipped `config/mod_config.json` replaces `characters` with `cards`:

| Group | Mod directory | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `ui` | `ui/` | UI textures, themes |
| `backgrounds` | `backgrounds/` | Scene backgrounds |
| `avatars` | `avatars/` | Character portraits |
| `characters` | `characters/` | Character sprites (code default only) |
| `icons` | `icons/` | UI icons |
| `items` | `items/` | Item sprites |
| `cards` | `cards/` | Battle action card images (shipped config) |
| `effects` | `effects/` | Effect textures |
| `skills` | `skills/` | Skill card artwork |

### `cache` section

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `root` | string | Cache storage path (Godot user path, e.g. `"user://mod_cache/"`) |
| `max_sizes` | object | Per-category cache size limits (LRU eviction) |

### `max_sizes` entries

Each factory type has a maximum cache capacity. When exceeded, the least recently used entry is evicted. The code default for `max_sizes` is an **empty dictionary** — the values below ship in `config/mod_config.json` and override the fallback capacities each factory passes to `CacheConfig.GetCacheSize`.

| Factory | Default | Caches |
|---|---|---|
| `avatars` | 64 | Avatar textures |
| `backgrounds` | 64 | Background textures |
| `items` | 64 | Item textures |
| `ui/icons` | 64 | UI icon textures |
| `ui` | 64 | General UI textures |
| `music` | 64 | Music audio streams |
| `sounds` | 64 | Sound effect audio |
| `actions` | 128 | Character action definitions |
| `characters` | 128 | Character definitions |
| `recipes` | 128 | Recipe definitions |

The first user edit of the catalog source list creates `user://catalog_servers.json`. That list then supplies enabled catalog URLs and token paths, including an intentionally empty list; shipped `catalog_urls` is the seed before the first edit. See Catalog configuration.

## How textures are discovered

1. `ModManager` scans each mod's directory
2. For each `texture_groups` entry (e.g. `"avatars"`), it looks in `mods/{mod}/avatars/`
3. Files matching `texture_extensions` (`.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`) are registered in `ModRegistry`
4. Factories (`AvatarFactory`, `BackgroundFactory`, etc.) resolve paths through the registry with caching

===== DOC docs/mods/crafting/index.md: Crafting stations =====
`crafting` adds workbenches that exchange materials for products. It uses the `recipe` kind supplied by `items_base` and adds `craft_station`. A station offers its listed recipes without requiring the player to unlock them first. Cooking is a separate mechanic with its own UI and recipe availability.

```json
{
  "Kind": "craft_station",
  "Items": [{
    "Id": "my_game/workbench", "Name": "my_game/workbench/name",
    "Recipes": ["my_game/salve"]
  }]
}
```

Put this in `stations/`, and define the recipe in `recipes/`:

```json
{
  "Kind": "recipe",
  "Items": [{
    "Id": "my_game/salve", "Name": "my_game/salve/name",
    "Description": "my_game/salve/description", "Time": 5,
    "Input": [{"Id": "rpg_plus/mushroom", "Count": 2}],
    "Output": [{"Id": "rpg_plus/salve", "Count": 1}]
  }]
}
```

This example depends on `crafting` and the `rpg_plus_game` content defining the input/output items. Names and descriptions are locale keys. `Time` advances the game clock in minutes.

```scenario
action craft.open station=my_game/workbench
end
```

The window shows recipes, owned/required materials and products. Craft is available when the complete recipe is valid and ingredients are sufficient. The service validates all items and positive counts before removing anything. Materials are consumed only on Craft. The world cannot be walked while the window is open; Esc closes it.

Products are ordinary inventory entries saved by `items_base`; the station does not own another inventory or save slot. The Data Editor edits stations and recipes from the schemas provided by `crafting`.

Implementation: `mods/crafting/src/`, `mods/crafting/schemas/crafting.json`. The `woodland` game suite covers the collection-to-crafting loop.

===== DOC docs/mods/editor_tools/index.md: Developer Tools =====
The developer tools system allows mods to create custom editor windows and register them in the in-game toolbar. Tools are discovered automatically via reflection, so any mod with C# code can contribute new tool windows without modifying the kernel.

## Architecture

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    subgraph Discovery
        TF[ToolFactory] -->|Reflections.FindAndCreate| ITP[IToolProvider implementations]
        TBF[ToolBoxButtonFactory] -->|Reflections.FindAndCreate| ITBB[IToolBoxButton implementations]
    end

    subgraph Kernel
        TB[ToolBox] -->|GetVisible| TBF
    end

    subgraph Runtime
        TW[ToolWindow popup]
        TB -->|AddToolButtons| BTN[Dynamic toolbar buttons]
        BTN -->|"Pressed: Get + ShowWindow(CreateWindow())"| TW
    end

    subgraph Mod Assembly
        MTP[MyToolProvider : IToolProvider]
        MTB[MyToolBoxButton : IToolBoxButton]
    end

    ITP --> MTP
    ITBB --> MTB
```

Tools bootstrapping happens at startup:

1. `ToolFactory.Instance` scans all loaded assemblies for `IToolProvider` implementations via `Reflections.FindAndCreate`.
2. `ToolBoxButtonFactory.Instance` scans for `IToolBoxButton` implementations.
3. When a mod assembly loads (`ModAssemblyLoader`), both factories call `RegisterAssembly(assembly)` to discover mod-provided tools.
4. `ToolBox` dynamically creates HUD toolbar buttons from `ToolBoxButtonFactory.GetVisible()`.

## Core interfaces

### `IToolProvider`

The entry point for a custom tool. Every tool window must have a provider that implements this interface.

| Member | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Id` | `string` | Unique identifier used as the registry key (e.g. `"scenario_editor"`) |
| `LocaleKey` | `string` | Translation key for the tool's display name, resolved via `TranslationServer.Translate` |
| `CreateWindow()` | `Window` | Creates a new `Window` instance for this tool. The caller is responsible for adding it to the scene tree and displaying it |

### `IToolBoxButton`

The in-game launcher interface. Implement it alongside `IToolProvider` to add a persistent button on the in-game HUD toolbar that opens your tool.

| Member | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `ToolId` | `string` | Matches an `IToolProvider.Id` in `ToolFactory`, wiring the button to a specific tool |
| `IconPath` | `string` | Resource path to the icon texture loaded for this button |
| `LocaleKey` | `string` | Translation key for the button's tooltip or accessible label |
| `IsVisible()` | `bool` | Return `false` to hide the button dynamically (e.g. dev-only buttons in release builds) |

`ToolBox` groups visible tools under one toolbar popup, labelled with each entry's translated `LocaleKey`. `ToolId` opens the provider through `ToolWindow.ShowWindow(tool.CreateWindow())`. `IconPath` is retained in the contract; the grouped popup currently uses text entries.

## `ToolWindow` base class

Abstract base class for tool popup windows. Extends Godot's `Window`. Key features:

- **Custom chrome** — the native border is removed and replaced with a custom
  title bar (title label, maximize toggle, close button) plus drag-to-move on
  the title bar and dedicated edge/corner resize zones around all four sides.
- **Maximize toggle** — via the title-bar button or double-clicking the title;
  maximized fills the game viewport, restore returns to the previous geometry.
- **Sizing limits** — `MinSize` is 800×400; `MaxSize` equals the game viewport
  size, so tool windows never exceed the game window.
- **Dirty-state tracking** — `SetDirty()` / `MarkClean()` add/remove a `*` prefix on the title bar. On close with unsaved changes, a confirmation dialog appears automatically.
- **Confirmation dialogs** — `ShowUnsavedDialog(Action)` shows a localized "unsaved changes" prompt; `ConfirmDiscardUnsaved()` provides a return-value variant.
- **Layout helpers**:
  - `CreateFillContainer()` — full-anchor `VBoxContainer` filling the entire window
  - `CreateToolbar()` — pre-configured `HBoxContainer` with 8px spacing
  - `CreateSidebar(VBoxContainer, int width = 280)` — fixed-width `PanelContainer` with vertical scrolling
- **Monospace font** — `GetMonospaceFont()` resolves a monospace font for code displays, falling back to `ThemeDB.FallbackFont`.
- **Static openers** — `Open<T>()` (generic, parameterless constructor) and `ShowWindow(Window)` (for `IToolProvider`-created instances) center the window on screen. Both apply the chrome via `SetupChrome()` before showing.
- **Teardown hook** — override the virtual `Cleanup()` to release resources; it is called automatically from `_ExitTree` when the window leaves the tree.

## Registry

### `ToolFactory`

Singleton registry (`ToolFactory.Instance`) for `IToolProvider` implementations.

| Method | Description |
|---|---|
| `Register(IToolProvider)` | Adds a tool; duplicates by `Id` are silently ignored (first-wins) |
| `RegisterAssembly(Assembly)` | Scans an assembly for `IToolProvider` implementations and registers them — the primary hook for mods |
| `Get(string id)` | Looks up a tool by `Id`, returns `null` if not found |
| `GetAll()` | Returns all registered tools as a read-only list — used for tool discovery and automation |

Logging uses the `"TOOLS"` category.

### `ToolBoxButtonFactory`

Singleton registry (`ToolBoxButtonFactory.Instance`) for `IToolBoxButton` implementations.

| Method | Description |
|---|---|
| `Register(IToolBoxButton)` | Adds a button definition (duplicates not filtered — same `ToolId` may have different visibility rules) |
| `RegisterAssembly(Assembly)` | Scans an assembly for `IToolBoxButton` implementations — the primary hook for mods |
| `GetVisible()` | Returns all registered buttons that pass `IsVisible()`, used by `ToolBox.AddToolButtons` |

## HUD integration — `ToolBox`

The `ToolBox` control (`mods/ui_base/src/Tool/ToolBox.cs`) is the main toolbar/HUD container. It is
manifest-driven and knows no concrete panels itself: `UiBuilder` hands it one
`UiEntry` per mod-declared toolbar panel (from `UiRegistry.PanelEntries`, pre-sorted by
button index, mod load order, then id), and `ApplyPanelButtons(entries)` builds a styled
button per entry — icon via `UIIconFactory`, label from the entry's `button.locale` key —
whose press calls `UiRegistry.ToggleEntry(id)` to show/hide the panel.

Tool windows ride the same bar: during `_Ready()`, `AddToolButtons(container)` appends one
button per `ToolBoxButtonFactory.GetVisible()` definition — label from `LocaleKey`, icon
from `IconPath` when the resource exists — and opens its tool via
`ToolWindow.ShowWindow(tool.CreateWindow())`, so the window gets the full chrome
(title bar, resize zones) and is centered rather than being shown raw from
`CreateWindow()` alone.

## Built-in tools

The `editor_tools` compatibility package installs seven independent tool mods. Select individual tools through `dev_tools`, or depend on `tools_base` for shared forms and file services. Scenario parsing is isolated in `scenario_tools_base`. No tool adds a title-screen button.

| Mod | Role |
|---|---|
| `tools_base` | Shared UI, entity/schema forms, locale editing, neutral scene/file services; no windows |
| `scenario_tools_base` | Scenario files, syntax and graph models; no windows |
| `scene_editor` | Logical scenes, backgrounds, ambient sound and scenario bindings |

The window tools are:

| Tool ID | Description | Locale Key |
|---|---|---|
| `scenario_editor` | Full editor for `.scenario` DSL files — file tree, text/graph tabs, metadata inspection, background thumbnail previews, play-test mode | `tools/scenario_editor/title` |
| `scene_builder` | Interactive Godot scene inspector — wrapper template picker, live viewport with node hierarchy tree, C# code editor with compile-and-run | `tools/scene_builder/title` |
| `light_editor` | Edits static lights on backgrounds — light list (max 10), per-light radius/influence/color editors, draggable handles, shader preview, JSON export/import | `tools/light_editor/title` |
| `interactive_editor` | Creates/edits interactive object definitions — BG preview with draggable collider handles (rect, circle, polygon), JSON output, load/save to mod directories | `tools/interactive_editor/title` |
| `map_editor` | Visual world-map zone editor — canvas with draggable regions/markers/labels per scene, locked/hidden flags, per-zone title keys and images, load/save of the zone JSON (see Map & Zones) | `tools/map_editor/title` |
| `catalog_export` | Exports a catalog entry (`catalog.json`) for a mod — download URL, category, tags, changelog, status, min game version, icon URL | `mods/catalog_title` |

The scenario editor's play-test works inside a running game too: **Stop**
brings the player back to the scene they were in (or to the tool's own route,
or to the main menu when the editor was opened from there), and a scene switch
made by the played scenario itself ends the play-test where it is instead of
throwing the player to the main menu.

Each tool has a provider class (`ScenarioEditorToolProvider`, `SceneBuilderToolProvider`, `LightEditorToolProvider`, `InteractiveEditorToolProvider`, `MapEditorToolProvider`, `CatalogExportToolProvider`) implementing `IToolProvider`, and a dedicated window class extending `ToolWindow`.

Every window tool provides an `IToolBoxButton` for the in-game toolbar. Studio instead declares its panel button in `manifest.ui`.

## Data editors: items and characters

Two tool mods build on the `tools_base` window `EntityEditorWindow`, each depending only on
the data it edits, so a game takes the ones it needs:

| Mod | Tool ID | Edits | Locale Key |
|---|---|---|---|
| `items_editor` | `items_editor` | items (`"Kind": "item"`): name and description, price, icon, types, use action | `tools/items_editor/title` |
| `characters_editor` | `characters_editor` | characters (`"Kind": "character"`): name, portrait, RPG charset, stats, battle actions | `tools/characters_editor/title` |

Both open from the in-game toolbar, and are part of the `cthangover`
developer tools and the optional `data_editor` mechanic of `rpg_plus`. Fields other mods' schemas add to
the kind appear in a "Fields of other mods" section (schemas).

- **Pick a mod** (any writable one) — its entities of the kind are listed with a search and their
  pictures, wherever their files are in the mod.
- **The form** shows the name in every language the mod has (`locale/*.properties`; a new entity's
  key is made from its id), pictures are picked from a grid of the mod folders, types and actions
  are check boxes. **New** (Ctrl+N), **Copy** (Ctrl+D), **Delete**, Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y.
- **Other fields** shows every property the form does not know (another mod's `heal` or `verb`, a
  character's `Loot`) as JSON; it is edited there and written back as it is, so the editor never
  drops data.
- **Save** (Ctrl+S) writes only the files that changed — new entities go to
  `items/<mod>.json` / `characters/<mod>.json` — and the running game re-reads its data and texts
  at once.

## RPG and schema authoring

- **RPG Map Editor** (`rpg_editor`) paints ground/structure, collision and atmosphere masks; places characters and props; creates map-owned Event rectangles; edits spawn, interactions and teleport links; supports rectangle selection, copy/paste, undo and Play from here. Map event size is `Width`/`Height`; a legacy invisible placement uses `Footprint.Size`.
- **RPG objects** (`rpg_object_editor`) edits the four forms, PNG imports, parts, footprints, anchors, states, variants, lights and routes. Its visual parts workspace moves/resizes art and sorting points, with behind/in-front checks using a reference character. Apply transfers the draft to the object; Save writes the mod.
- **Data Editor and command palette** (`schema_tools`) use all loaded schemas, including typed lists, numeric choices, quest stages and equipment-type pickers. **Rename…** previews local reference changes and foreign usages; Undo rename restores the local transaction.
- **Translations** (`locale_editor`) lists missing translations from the locale atlas and writes each text into the owning mod's flat locale files.
- **Effects Editor** (`effects_editor`) previews atmosphere parameters; its map button opens the map editor's Effects mask tool.
- **Scenario Studio** (`scenario_studio`) supports modal pause/step/breakpoints, rollback, live edits and export of the game's studio layer. The **Background** tab separately controls concurrent NPC runs. `self.` flags in the State page can be edited in the running game.

Items and Characters editors can include dependency entries and save minimal patches in the selected writable mod. Field reset inherits the owner's value again. Characters can create a companion join interaction; a selected shared scenario plays before the appended join commands. Items can open the RPG map editor's pickup placement through the optional capability interface. Editors add needed resource-owner dependencies on Save; transitive dependencies already supplied by the chosen base are not duplicated.

See RPG, map events, schemas and background scenarios for the formats and limitations. Bundled mod guides in Mods & Collections show the actual authoring windows.

## Pages

- Creating a Custom Tool — step-by-step guide with code examples and locale keys

===== DOC docs/mods/editor_tools/tutorial.md: Creating a Custom Tool =====
Depend on `tools_base>=1` and `ui_base>=1`, and include the new `.cs` files in `manifest.sources`. Implement a tool with three classes, each in its own file. Tool windows are launched from the in-game toolbar.

## 1. Provider — `IToolProvider`

```csharp
namespace MyMod
{
    using Cthangover.Core.UI.Tool;
    using Cthangover.Mods.Interface.Tool;
    using Godot;
    public class MyToolProvider : IToolProvider
    {
        public string Id => "my_tool";
        public string LocaleKey => "tools/my_tool/title";
        public Window CreateWindow() => new MyToolWindow();

    }
}
```

## 2. Window — extends `ToolWindow`

```csharp
namespace MyMod
{
    using Cthangover.Core.UI.Tool;
    using Cthangover.Mods.Interface.Tool;
    using Godot;
    public class MyToolWindow : ToolWindow
    {
        public MyToolWindow() : base("tools/my_tool/title")
        {
            var root = CreateFillContainer();
            AddChild(root);

            var toolbar = CreateToolbar();
            root.AddChild(toolbar);

            var closeBtn = new Button { Text = TranslationServer.Translate("tools/my_tool/close") };
            closeBtn.Pressed += () => QueueFree();
            toolbar.AddChild(closeBtn);
        }
    }
}
```

## 3. Toolbar entry — `IToolBoxButton`

```csharp
namespace MyMod
{
    using Cthangover.Core.UI.Tool;
    using Cthangover.Mods.Interface.Tool;
    using Godot;
    public class MyToolBoxButton : IToolBoxButton
    {
        public string ToolId => "my_tool";
        public string IconPath => "";
        public string LocaleKey => "tools/my_tool/title";
        public bool IsVisible() => true;
    }
}
```

## 4. Add locale keys

In your mod's `locale/ui_en.properties`:

```properties
tools/my_tool/title = My Custom Tool
tools/my_tool/close = Close
```

## Registration checklist

| Step | Action |
|---|---|
| 1 | Create provider class implementing `IToolProvider` with a unique `Id` |
| 2 | Create window class extending `ToolWindow` — use `CreateFillContainer`, `CreateToolbar`, etc. |
| 3 | Create an `IToolBoxButton` class for the toolbar tools list |
| 4 | Add locale keys in your mod's `locale/ui_en.properties` |
| 5 | Ensure your mod's `manifest.json` has `"sources"` covering the new `.cs` files |

The tool is auto-discovered — no manual registration needed. The provider and button classes are found via reflection when the mod assembly loads.

## Locale keys reference

Common UI locale keys used by the tool system:

| Key | Context |
|---|---|
| `tools/unsaved_title` | Unsaved changes dialog title |
| `tools/unsaved_text` | Unsaved changes dialog body |

Tool-specific keys (`tools/<tool_id>/...`) live in the tool mod's own locale files.

## Related

- Source Code in Mods — C# compilation and mod bootstrapping
- Scenes — scene lifecycle and how scenes reference tools
- Interactive Objects — `interactive_editor` creates these definitions
- Locale — `.properties` files for tool UI strings

===== DOC docs/mods/effects/index.md: Effects and atmosphere profiles =====
`effects_base` supplies scene effects and continuous atmosphere. A scenario's `effect <id>` loads an effect scene from `effects/`. Atmosphere uses a separate `atmosphere` entity with emitter data; it can cover a map or a painted subset of cells and is not a scenario command.

## A profile

```json
{
  "Kind": "atmosphere",
  "Items": [{
    "Id": "my_game/ash", "Name": "my_game/ash/name",
    "Emitters": [{
      "Type": "particles", "Shape": "flake", "Count": 80,
      "Color": "#a0a0a070", "Velocity": [0.1, 0.02, -0.4],
      "Height": [0.1, 5], "Size": [0.5, 1.2], "Sway": 0.2, "Seed": 17
    }]
  }]
}
```

Put profiles in `atmospheres/`. A map's `Atmosphere` selects a whole-map profile. Its `Effects` list adds profiles, optionally with `Cells` masks. RPG Map Editor's **Effects** tool paints and erases masks; Effects Editor edits profile parameters and opens the map editor with its profile selected. Removing the last cell removes that mask placement. Masks are implemented in model v2.

`particles` supports `flake`, `leaf`, `glow`, `dust`, `streak`. Velocity is XYZ world units per second, positive Z up; Height is a world-unit range and Size a pixel-radius range. `Pulse` fades periodically and `Trail` controls streak duration. Counts are capped at 512 per emitter and 2048 per profile. `ripples` draws projected rings in a named `Region`; the tile host supplies `water`. A missing region draws nothing. Cosmetic randomness is separate from gameplay randomness.

## Extending and hosting

Implement `IAtmosphereEmitterFactory` in a mod depending on `effects_base`. Its `Id` selects JSON `Type`; `Create` receives `AtmosphereEmitterInfo`, including custom `ExtraFields`, and returns an `IAtmosphereEmitter`. `Advance` updates once per host frame; `Draw` draws through the context projection without advancing; `Dispose` releases owned assets. Factories are discovered and removed through the mod pipeline. Duplicate types, unknown types and invalid ranges are diagnosed.

Outside an RPG map, host an `AtmosphereView`, call `Configure(profileId, context)` with bounds and a `Func<Vector3, Vector2>` projection, then `Advance(delta)` each frame. Call `Release()` before manual freeing; tree exit also releases resources. There is no RPG dependency in the atmosphere engine. Scene exit, renderer replacement and profile changes release owned emitters; compatible geometry edits retain animation state.

Implementation: `mods/effects_base/src/Atmosphere/`, `mods/effects_editor/`, the atmosphere schema and `mods/rpg/src/Model/RpgMap.cs`.

===== DOC docs/mods/events/index.md: Event Bus =====
The **ModEventBus** is a global publish-subscribe event bus that allows mods to react
to game events and communicate with each other without direct dependencies.

It is registered as a Godot autoload, accessible from both C# and GDScript.

## Built-in engine events

The engine publishes these events automatically. Each event has a string key
(GDScript layer) and a typed C# class.

### Lifecycle events (ordered by priority)

| Event | Key | Priority | Fires when |
|-------|-----|----------|------------|
| `SceneExitedEvent` | `"scene_exited"` | lifecycle | Leaving the current scene |
| `SceneEnteredEvent` | `"scene_entered"` | lifecycle | New scene loaded, root in tree |
| `BattleStartedEvent` | `"battle_started"` | lifecycle | Battle core initializes *(declared by `turn_battle_base`)* |
| `BattleEndedEvent` | `"battle_ended"` | lifecycle | Battle ends (victory/defeat) *(declared by `turn_battle_base`)* |

Lifecycle events support subscriber **priorities**: lower numbers fire first.
Engine subscribers use these priorities:

| Subscriber | Priority | Purpose |
|------------|----------|---------|
| `SceneManager` internals | 0 | Scene background (`ApplySceneDefaults`) |
| `SceneManager` internals | 5 | Audio (`PlaySceneAmbient`) |
| `SceneManager` internals | 10 | Music (`UpdateMusicType`) |
| `UiHost` | 30 | Shows/hides scene-scoped manifest `ui` elements on enter/exit |
| `SceneContextNode` | 0 (default) | Clears cached node lookups and interactive objects on `scene_exited` |

Note that scenario event composition (`ComposeEvents`) is **not** a bus subscriber — it runs synchronously inside `SceneManager.SwitchScene`.

### Game state events

| Event | Key | Payload |
|-------|-----|---------|
| `CharacterDiedEvent` | `"character_died"` | `character_id`, `character_name`, `source` ("battle"/"world") *(declared by `turn_battle_base`)* |
| `CharactersDefeatedEvent` | `"characters_defeated"` | `source`, `character_ids` — a group defeat (battle won, trap); subscribers like the recruiting mod resolve ids through `CharacterFactory` |
| `QuestStatusChangedEvent` | `"quest_status_changed"` | `quest_id`, `old_status`, `new_status` |
| `QuestTagChangedEvent` | `"quest_tag_changed"` | `quest_id`, `tag`, `added` (bool) |
| `QuestDataStatusChangedEvent` | `"quest_data_status_changed"` | `old`, `new` — numeric quest progress counter; carries no quest id (a change marker for condition caches) |
| `QuestStateRestoredEvent` | `"quest_state_restored"` | (empty) — published once after a save's quest state is fully restored |
| `FlagChangedEvent` | `"flag_changed"` | `key`, `value` — a scenario condition flag changed (`ScenarioCondition.SetFlag`) |
| `InteractiveStateChangedEvent` | `"interactive_state_changed"` | `id` — an interactive object was removed (not published on add, so `interactive_add` re-runs stay idempotent) |
| `MapZoneChangedEvent` | `"map_zone_changed"` | `zone_id` (empty for bulk changes), `change` — a map zone's open/visible state changed |
| `ConditionStateChangedEvent` | `"condition_state_changed"` | `version` — something scenario conditions read changed (any of the quest/flag/interactive/map events above, or `ConditionStateMonitor.MarkStateChanged`); event chains start the next scenario from it. May arrive mid-change: read state in a deferred call, not in the handler |
| `InventoryChangedEvent` | `"inventory_changed"` | `item_id`, `delta`, `new_count` |
| `TimeTickEvent` | `"time_tick"` | `time_tick`, `normalized_time` |
| `NewGameStartedEvent` | `"new_game_started"` | (empty) — published by `GameData.StartNewGame` after runtime state is wiped; mods re-seed per-session data here |
| `RecruitJoinedEvent` | `"recruit_joined"` | `recruit_id`, `recruit_name` *(declared by the `recruiting` mod, not yet published)* |
| `RecruitLeftEvent` | `"recruit_left"` | `recruit_id`, `recruit_name` *(declared by the `recruiting` mod, not yet published)* |

### UI events

| Event | Key | Payload |
|-------|-----|---------|
| `DialogStartedEvent` | `"dialog_started"` | `dialog_id`, `speaker` *(declared, not yet published by the engine)* |
| `DialogEndedEvent` | `"dialog_ended"` | `locker_id` — id of the event (scenario) that ran the dialog, empty for an interactive's click dialog; published exactly once per finished dialog (not on a studio restart). `dialog_id` is declared but not filled yet |
| `NotificationRequestedEvent` | `"notification_requested"` | `text`, `category` |
| `ShellReadyEvent` | `"shell_ready"` | `root` — the UI shell finished building; `UiHost` attaches mod panels on this event |

### Mod lifecycle events

| Event | Key | Payload |
|-------|-----|---------|
| `ModLoadedEvent` | `"mod_loaded"` | `mod_id` |
| `ModResourcesReadyEvent` | `"mod_resources_ready"` | (empty) |

## C# API

### Subscribe to a typed event

```csharp
using Cthangover.Core.Events;

ModEventBus.Subscribe<SceneEnteredEvent>(e =>
{
    GameLogger.Log("MY_MOD", $"Scene entered: {e.SceneName}");
}, priority: 0, modId: "my_mod");
```

- `handler` — callback receiving the typed event.
- `priority` — only meaningful for `[LifecycleEvent]` classes (lower = earlier). Default 0.
- `modId` — optional, used for cleanup via `ClearForMod(modId)` on unload.

### Publish a custom event

```csharp
[EventKey("my_mod:custom_event")]
public class MyCustomEvent : ModEvent
{
    public string Data { get; init; }
}

// Publish:
ModEventBus.Publish(new MyCustomEvent
{
    SenderModId = "my_mod",
    Data = "hello"
});
```

Other mods subscribe by:

```csharp
ModEventBus.Subscribe<MyCustomEvent>(e =>
{
    GameLogger.Log("OTHER_MOD", $"Received: {e.Data}");
});
```

GDScript mods subscribe via the string key `"my_mod:custom_event"`.

### Unsubscribe

```csharp
Action<MyCustomEvent> handler = e => { /* ... */ };
ModEventBus.Subscribe(handler);
// later:
ModEventBus.Unsubscribe(handler);
```

## GDScript API

### Auto-registration (convention-based)

GDScript objects loaded by `ModGDScriptLoader` are automatically scanned for
methods starting with `on_mod_event_`. Each such method is subscribed to the
string event bus under the key after the prefix.

```gdscript
# scripts/my_handler.gd
func on_mod_event_scene_entered(data: Dictionary) -> void:
    print("Entered scene: " + data.scene_name)

func on_mod_event_battle_ended(data: Dictionary) -> void:
    print("Battle ended: " + data.result)

func on_mod_event_cooking_meal_ready(data: Dictionary) -> void:
    if data.dish == "wolf_stew":
        boost_morale()
```

The method receives a `Dictionary` with snake_case keys (e.g. `scene_name`,
`item_id`, `old_status`) matching the typed event properties.

### Manual subscription

```gdscript
ModEventBus.subscribe("scene_entered", _on_scene, 0, "my_mod")

func _on_scene(data: Dictionary) -> void:
    print(data.scene_name)
```

### Publishing from scenario actions

GDScript scenario actions can publish events via `ctx.PublishEvent()`:

```gdscript
func get_name() -> String:
    return "cooking.meal_cooked"

func run(ctx) -> void:
    ctx.PublishEvent("cooking_meal_ready", {
        "dish": ctx.GetParam("dish"),
        "quality": ctx.GetParam("quality")
    })
```

### Publishing directly

```gdscript
ModEventBus.publish("cooking_meal_ready", { "dish": "wolf_stew", "quality": 0.8 })
```

## Error isolation

Each handler is executed in a try/catch. An error in one handler does **not**
prevent other handlers from executing. Errors are logged to the `EVENT_BUS` category.

## Cleanup

When a mod is reloaded or unloaded, its subscriptions are removed:

```csharp
ModEventBus.Instance.ClearForMod("my_mod");
```

This covers both typed (C#) and string (GDScript) subscriptions for that mod.
GDScript mods automatically get `ClearForMod` called on reload via `ModGDScriptLoader`.

## Typed layer reference

The typed C# layer lives in `Core/Events/ModEventBus.cs`. All three methods are static and constrained to `T : ModEvent`:

| Method | Signature | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `Subscribe<T>` | `Subscribe<T>(Action<T> handler, int priority = 0, string modId = null)` | `priority` is only meaningful for `[LifecycleEvent]` classes (lower = earlier); ignored otherwise |
| `Unsubscribe<T>` | `Unsubscribe<T>(Action<T> handler)` | Removes a single typed handler |
| `Publish<T>` | `Publish<T>(T evt)` | Calls all typed handlers first, then auto-bridges to the string layer |

### Auto-bridge via `[EventKey]`

On `Publish<T>`, the bus reads the event class's `[EventKey("snake_case_key")]` attribute (`Core/Events/EventKeyAttribute.cs`) and broadcasts a `Dictionary` on that key so GDScript subscribers receive the same event. Public instance properties **declared on the event class itself** become snake_case keys (`SceneName` → `scene_name`); inherited `ModEvent` members are not included, and `Node` references pass through unwrapped. An event class without `[EventKey]` does not bridge — only typed C# subscribers receive it.

### `[LifecycleEvent]` and delivery order

Marking an event class with `[LifecycleEvent]` (`Core/Events/LifecycleEventAttribute.cs`) enables priority-ordered delivery: every `Subscribe<T>` call re-sorts that event's subscriber list by `priority`, so lower numbers fire first. Without the attribute, handlers run in subscription order.

### Base class `ModEvent`

All typed events derive from `ModEvent` (`Core/Events/ModEvent.cs`), which carries two members available to every handler:

| Member | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `SenderModId` | `string` | Sending mod (`null` = engine) |
| `Tick` | `long` | Game-time tick at publish time (from `GameData.Instance.Runtime.Time`); `0` if `GameData` is not yet initialized |

### Cleanup API

- `ClearForMod(string modId)` — removes all subscriptions registered with that `modId`, across both the typed and string layers. Called on mod unload/reload.
- `ClearAll()` — wipes every subscription on both layers.

## Reload and background speech events

`ScenarioContentChangedEvent` tells consumers to re-read changed scenario content. `ModSetReloadedEvent` reports an applied/rejected mod-set change; consumers must inspect `Applied` before rebuilding live state. Their contracts live in `Core/Events/Events/`.

`npc_barks_base` declares `BarkSpeechEvent`, bridged under `npc_speech`. It carries `EventId`, `SessionId`, simulation time `At`, `RunId`, `UtteranceId`, placement `Speaker`, `Map`, `Cue`, `Phase` and `Depth`. Phases are `speech.started`, `speech.finished`, `speech.interrupted`. The director defers cue reactions until the next simulation update and filters stale or duplicate events; interruption is not reported as completion. Events are transient and are not replayed from saves. See Background NPC scenarios.

===== DOC docs/mods/interactives/index.md: Interactive Objects =====
Interactive objects are clickable scene elements with hover effects and custom actions. They are added to scenes from scenario scripts via `interactive_add` and defined as JSON files.

## JSON structure

```jsonc
{
  "Items": [{                                              // Array of interactive object definitions
    "id": "kitchen_door",                                  // Unique identifier for interactive_add / interactive_remove commands
    "texture": "interactives/kitchen_door",                // Texture key resolved via ModManager.ResolveTexture
    "layer": "foreground",                                 // Visual layer: background, foreground, or ui
    "zIndex": 10,                                          // Z-ordering within the layer (higher = on top)
    "enabled": true,                                       // Whether the object responds to input
    "visible": true,                                       // Whether the object is rendered
    "cursor": "PointingHand",                              // Mouse cursor override (Godot CursorShape enum name)
    "hitArea": {                                           // Collider shape and position in normalised 0..1 coordinates
      "type": "rect",                                      // Shape type: rect, circle, or polygon
      "x": 0.7,                                            // X position (top-left for rect, centre for circle)
      "y": 0.3,                                            // Y position
      "width": 0.15,                                       // Width (rect type only)
      "height": 0.5                                        // Height (rect type only)
    },
    "highlight": {                                         // Visual highlight settings on hover
      "color": "#FFFF0033",                                // Modulate colour as hex string
      "scale": 1.02,                                       // Pulse scale multiplier on hover (1.02 = 2% bigger)
      "duration": 0.15                                     // Animation duration in seconds for hover/unhover
    },
    "actions": {                                           // Actions triggered by pointer events
      "onClick": {                                         // Action on mouse click
        "scenario": "scenarios/home/kitchen_door.scenario" // Path to scenario file to run on click
      },
      "onHoverEnter": "",                                     // Inline DSL commands executed on mouse enter
      "onHoverLeave": ""                                   // Inline DSL commands executed on mouse leave
    }
  }]
}
```

## Field reference

### Top-level fields

| Field | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | *(required)* | Unique identifier used in `interactive_add` / `interactive_remove` commands |
| `texture` | string | *(required)* | Texture key resolved via `ModManager.ResolveTexture`. Full-screen with alpha |
| `layer` | string | `"foreground"` | Visual layer: `"background"`, `"foreground"`, or `"ui"` |
| `zIndex` | int | 0 | Z-ordering within the layer (higher = on top) |
| `enabled` | bool | `true` | Whether the object responds to input |
| `visible` | bool | `true` | Whether the object is rendered |
| `cursor` | string | — | Mouse cursor override (Godot `CursorShape` enum name, e.g. `"PointingHand"`) |
| `hitArea` | object | `{ type: "rect" }` | Collider shape and position |
| `highlight` | object | — | Visual highlight settings on hover |
| `actions` | object | — | Actions triggered by pointer events |

### `hitArea` fields

Coordinates are in **normalised viewport coordinates (0..1)**, where `(0,0)` = top-left, `(1,1)` = bottom-right.

| Field | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | string | `"rect"` | Shape type: `"rect"`, `"circle"`, or `"polygon"` |
| `x` | float | 0 | X position (top-left for rect, centre for circle) |
| `y` | float | 0 | Y position |
| `width` | float | 0.1 | Width for rect type |
| `height` | float | 0.1 | Height for rect type |
| `radius` | float | 0.05 | Radius for circle type |
| `vertices` | array | — | `[{x, y}, ...]` polygon vertices (normalised) |

### `highlight` fields

| Field | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `color` | string | `"#FFFF0033"` | Modulate colour as a hex string (e.g. `"#FF000044"`) |
| `scale` | float | 1.02 | Pulse scale multiplier on hover (e.g. 1.02 = 2% bigger) |
| `duration` | float | 0.15 | Animation duration in seconds for hover/unhover |

### `actions` fields

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `onClick` | `ClickAction` — path to `.scenario` and/or inline DSL commands |
| `onHoverEnter` | Inline DSL commands executed on mouse enter |
| `onHoverLeave` | Inline DSL commands executed on mouse leave |

### `onClick` sub-fields

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `scenario` | Path to a `.scenario` file to run on click |
| `commands` | Array of inline DSL command strings to execute on click |

## Scenario commands

| Command | Description |
|---|---|
| `interactive_add object_id` | Instantiate an interactive object by its definition ID |
| `interactive_remove object_id` | Remove an interactive object |
| `interactive_set object_id enabled=true\|false` | Enable/disable or show/hide an object. Named params: `enabled`, `visible` |
| `interactive_clear` | Remove all interactive objects from the scene |

## Directory structure

```filestree
interactives/
├── kitchen_door.json
└── treasure_chest.json
```

There is no index file. `InteractiveResolver.CollectInteractiveDefsRecursive` scans every `*.json` file under the mod's `interactives/` folder recursively (including subfolders) and reads each one as a `{"Items": [...]}` envelope.

## Adding a new interactive object

1. Create `interactives/{id}.json` with the full definition — it will be picked up automatically
2. In a scenario, activate it: `interactive_add my_object`

===== DOC docs/mods/items/index.md: Items =====
JSON-defined items — consumables, resources, and recipe items — with localization, pricing, and crafting recipes.

## JSON structure

```jsonc
{
  // Unique item ID in path-style format (e.g. "food/ration")
  "Id":          "type/item_id",
  // Locale key for the item's display name
  "Name":        "locale_key_for_name",
  // Locale key for the item's tooltip/description
  "Description": "locale_key_for_description",
  // Base purchase price in the game economy
  "Cost":        5,
  // Path to the item icon sprite, resolved under the mod's sprites directory
  "Sprite":      "type/item_id",
  // Type flags (comma-separated): Food, Resource, Quest, Recipe, Used, TargetUsed
  "ItemType":    "Food,Resource",
  // Action ID for the item's use effect; resolved via IItemAction factory
  "ItemAction":  "action_id"
}
```

## Field reference

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `Id` | Unique item ID, path-style (`"food/ration"`) |
| `Name` | Locale key resolved from `.properties` files |
| `Description` | Locale key for tooltip/info text |
| `Cost` | Base price in the game economy |
| `Sprite` | Path to the item icon under the mod's sprites directory |
| `ItemType` | Type tag(s), comma-separated: `Food`, `Resource`, `Quest`, `Recipe`, `Used`, `TargetUsed` |
| `ItemAction` | Action ID resolved via `IItemAction` factory — can be a C# class or a GDScript with `get_item_action_id()` + `use(item)`. Defines the item's use effect |

### ItemType values

`ItemType` is a `[Flags]` enum — multiple values can be combined with a comma:

| Value | Hex | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Quest` | `0x01` | Quest item — tracked in quest log |
| `Used` | `0x02` | Self-targeting use item |
| `TargetUsed` | `0x04` | Ally/enemy-targeting use item |
| `Food` | `0x08` | Edible (consumes hunger stat) |
| `Resource` | `0x10` | Crafting material |
| `Recipe` | `0x20` | Recipe item (unlocks in cookbook) |
| `CantDrop` | `0x80000000` | Cannot be discarded from inventory |

### Extra JSON keys

Any JSON key that does not match a DTO field is captured by
`ItemInfo.ExtraProperties` (`[JsonExtensionData]`) and imported by
`ItemFactory` into `Item.Properties` — a string-keyed `PropertyData` bag
(`mods/items_base/src/ItemInfo.cs`, `mods/items_base/src/Item.cs`). Numbers import as int/float,
booleans as bool, strings as strings; arrays and objects are stored as raw JSON
text. Core code never reads `Properties` — the bag exists so your own C# item
actions or scenario logic can attach custom data to items without schema
changes.

```jsonc
{
  "Id": "food/ration",
  "...": "...",
  // Captured into Properties — readable from your own code via item.Properties.GetInt("MyMod/Saturation")
  "MyMod/Saturation": 3
}
```

## Item actions (IItemAction)

The `ItemAction` field references an implementation of `IItemAction`
(`Core/Items/IItemAction.cs`) — the item's on-use behaviour:

```csharp
public class MyHealAction : IItemAction
{
    // Registry lookup key — must match the item's "ItemAction" field in JSON
    public string ID => "mymod/heal";

    public bool UseAction(IItem item)
    {
        // Returning FALSE aborts consumption — the item stays in the
        // inventory and the UI can show a failure feedback instead
        return TryHeal(item);
    }
}
```

Registration options:

- **Auto-discovery** — on first use, `ItemActionFactory` scans every loaded
  assembly for public `IItemAction` implementations and indexes them by `ID`.
  A mod DLL just needs to contain the class — no registration code required.
- **Manual registration** — `ItemActionFactory.Register(IItemAction)` adds a
  single instance; `ItemActionFactory.RegisterAssembly(assembly)` scans one
  assembly (used for hot-loaded mod assemblies).

The special ID `"null"` (or an empty value) is an explicit no-action marker:
`ItemActionFactory.Get` returns `null` for it, meaning the item has no use
effect.

## Real examples

### Consumable — `cthangover_game/items/food/ration.json`

```jsonc
{
  // Unique item ID
  "Id": "food/ration",
  // Locale key → "Ration"
  "Name": "items/food/ration/name",
  // Locale key → "A daily ration unit for one person"
  "Description": "items/food/ration/desc",
  // Base price: 5 gold
  "Cost": 5,
  // Icon sprite path under the mod's sprites folder
  "Sprite": "food/ration",
  // Edible item (consumes hunger stat on use)
  "ItemType": "Food"
}
```

### Crafting material — `cthangover_game/items/food/wolf_meat.json`

```jsonc
{
  // Unique item ID
  "Id": "food/wolf_meat",
  // Locale key → "Wolf cutlet"
  "Name": "items/food/wolf_meat/name",
  // Locale key → "Raw wolf meat"
  "Description": "items/food/wolf_meat/desc",
  // Base price: 1 gold — cheap, but important for recipes
  "Cost": 1,
  // Icon sprite path
  "Sprite": "food/wolf_meat",
  // Both a crafting ingredient (Resource) and edible (Food)
  "ItemType": "Resource,Food"
}
```

Note: `ItemType` can be multiple values — `"Resource,Food"` means it's both a crafting ingredient and edible.

### Recipe item

Recipe items are items that represent a craftable recipe in the player's inventory:

```jsonc
{
  // Unique recipe item ID
  "Id": "recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration",
  // Locale key for the recipe name
  "Name": "recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration/name",
  // Locale key for the recipe description
  "Description": "recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration/desc",
  // Icon sprite path
  "Sprite": "recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration",
  // Recipe type — unlocks in the cookbook when acquired
  "ItemType": "Recipe"
}
```

## Item file format

Every item file is a self-describing envelope routed by `Kind` — the file may
live anywhere in the mod and there is no index file:

```jsonc
{
  // Entity kind — routing discriminator for the shared entity resolver
  "Kind": "item",
  "Items": [
    {
      // Unique item ID
      "Id": "food/ration",
      "Name": "items/food/ration/name",
      "Description": "items/food/ration/desc",
      "Sprite": "food/ration",
      "ItemType": "Food"
    }
  ]
}
```

## Recipes

Recipes are their own entity kind and follow the same envelope:

```jsonc
{
  // Recipes are separate entities, not items
  "Kind": "recipe",
  "Items": [{
    // Unique recipe ID
    "Id": "recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration",
    // Locale key for the recipe display name
    "Name": "recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration/name",
    // Locale key for the recipe description text
    "Description": "recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration/desc",
    // Workbench type — "Cooking" is currently the only supported type
    "Type": "Cooking",
    // Crafting time in in-game minutes
    "Time": 15,
    // Required ingredients: Id references an item definition, Count is the quantity needed
    "Input": [
      { "Id": "food/wolf_meat", "Count": 1 },
      { "Id": "resource/branches", "Count": 2 }
    ],
    // Produced items on successful craft
    "Output": [
      { "Id": "food/ration", "Count": 1 }
    ]
  }]
}
```

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `Id` | Unique recipe ID |
| `Name` | Locale key for recipe display name |
| `Description` | Locale key for the recipe description text |
| `Type` | Workbench type (`WorkbenchType` enum). The enum currently declares a single value: `"Cooking"` |
| `Time` | Crafting time in in-game minutes |
| `Input` | Required ingredients: `Id` (item ID) + `Count` |
| `Output` | Produced items: `Id` (item ID) + `Count` |

## Inventory API

The player inventory (`Inventory` / `IInventory`, `mods/items_base/src/Inventory.cs`)
is an ordered list of item slots:

- `Items` — `List<IItemContainer>`, each slot being `{ Item, Count }`. Slot
  order drives UI layout; empty containers are deleted when a stack hits zero.
- `Change` — C# event `Action<string itemId, int delta>` fired on every add/remove.

All query and mutation methods are overloaded by **item id string** or by
**`IItem` instance**:

| Method | Description |
|---|---|
| `HasItem(id \| item)` | Does any slot hold this item? |
| `TryGet(id \| item)` | Returns the `IItemContainer` slot, or null |
| `CheckCount(id \| item)` | Total count across slots (0 if absent) |
| `Add(id \| item, count = 1)` | Stacks onto an existing slot, or creates a new one by resolving the id through `ItemFactory`. Unknown ids are silently ignored |
| `Remove(id \| item, count = 1)` | Removes items; returns `false` without changes if the slot holds fewer than `count` |

Every successful mutation publishes an `InventoryChangedEvent` to the global
`ModEventBus` — published from `Inventory.PublishInventoryChanged`
(`mods/items_base/src/Inventory.cs`). GDScript subscribers receive it under the key
`"inventory_changed"` with a snake_case payload:

| Payload key | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `item_id` | string | Affected item ID |
| `delta` | int | Signed change amount (negative on removal) |
| `new_count` | int | Slot count after the mutation |

## GDScript bridge objects

GDScripts never touch `IInventory` directly — the engine hands them wrapper
objects:

### `GDInventoryService`

A `[GlobalClass]` `RefCounted` wrapping the inventory. Plain-id methods mirror
the C# API; the `*ByItem` variants take a `GDItem` instead of an id string:

| Method | Description |
|---|---|
| `HasItem(id)` / `HasItemByItem(gd_item)` | Item presence check |
| `CheckCount(id)` / `CheckCountByItem(gd_item)` | Stack size lookup |
| `Add(id, count = 1)` / `AddByItem(gd_item, count = 1)` | Add items |
| `Remove(id, count = 1)` / `RemoveByItem(gd_item, count = 1)` | Remove items; returns success |

### `GDItem`

A `[GlobalClass]` snapshot passed as the `item` argument to a GDScript's
`use(item)`:

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Id` | string | Item identifier |
| `Name` | string | Display name |
| `Description` | string | Tooltip text |
| `Cost` | int | Purchase price |
| `ItemType` | int | `ItemType` flags as int |
| *(internal)* | — | The wrapper keeps an internal reference to the real `IItem`; `Sprite` and `Properties` are deliberately not exposed |

See GDScript in Mods → Items for the
authoring-side view.

## Locale keys

Item JSONs reference locale keys — you need to define the actual text in `locale/en.properties`:

```properties
items/food/ration/name = Ration
items/food/ration/desc = A daily ration unit for one person
items/food/wolf_meat/name = Wolf cutlet
items/food/wolf_meat/desc = Raw wolf meat
recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration/name = Ration from wolf meat
recipe/wolf_meat_to_ration/desc = A recipe for cooking wolf meat safely
```

## Adding a new item

The quickest way is the **Items** editor (`items_editor`, see Developer Tools):
**New**, type the name, pick the icon, Save. By hand:

1. Create a JSON file anywhere in your mod with the `{ "Kind": "item", "Items": [ ... ] }` envelope and all fields
2. Add locale keys for name and description
3. Optionally add a sprite icon
4. If the item has an active use, reference it via the factory — implement either an `IItemAction` C# class or a GDScript with `get_item_action_id()` + `use(item)`. See GDScript in Mods for details.

===== DOC docs/mods/load-order/index.md: Mod Load Order =====
The mod load order determines which mod's assets and data take priority when two or more
mods define the same resource. The system combines automatic dependency-based ordering with optional manual
user overrides.

## How ordering works

The game builds the final load order from three inputs, applied in strict priority:

```
1. depends (manifest.declared) → 2. Manual order (user preference) → 3. Alphabetical (deterministic fallback)
```

### Layer 1: `depends` — mandatory

Every mod may declare dependencies in `manifest.json` using **versioned dependency strings**:

```jsonc
{
  "id": "cooking",                                         // Canonical mod identifier
  "depends": ["cthangover_game>=1", "ui_base>=1"]                   // Versioned dependency strings
}
```

A dependency string has the format: `modId[operator][version][?]`

#### Operators

| String | Meaning | Example |
|--------|---------|---------|
| `cthangover_game>=1` | version ≥ 1.0.0 | Any core 1.x or higher |
| `cthangover_game=1.0.0` | exact match | Strictly 1.0.0 |
| `cthangover_game^1` | prefix match | 1.x.x — matches 1.0.0, 1.5.0, but not 2.0.0 |
| `cthangover_game>1` | greater than | Any version above 1.0.0 |
| `cthangover_game<2` | less than | Any version below 2.0.0 |
| `cthangover_game<=1` | less or equal | Version ≤ 1.0.0 |
| `cthangover_game` | any version | No version constraint |
| `cthangover_game>=1?` | optional | If core is installed, must be ≥1; no error if absent |

Version comparison is **segment-based** with zero-padding: `1` equals `1.0` equals `1.0.0`.
Segments may contain letters (e.g. `1a`, `alpha-3`). Each segment is compared as integer
if both sides are parseable, otherwise as string (`Ordinal`).

#### Optional dependencies (`?`)

Appending `?` to a dependency makes it **optional**:

```jsonc
{ "depends": ["optional_mod>=1?"] }                        // ? suffix makes this dependency optional
```

- If the optional mod is **not installed** → no error, the dependency is skipped.
- If the optional mod **is installed** → its version must satisfy the constraint.

This is useful for mods that want to react to another mod being present without
requiring it.

#### Strict equality (`=`) vs prefix match (`^`)

`=` requires **all** segments to match (with zero-padding):

- `cthangover_game=1.0` does **not** match `1.0.5` — the third segment `"0"` ≠ `"5"`.
- `cthangover_game=1.0.0` matches `1.0.0`.

`^` matches only the specified segments, ignoring the rest:

- `cthangover_game^1.0` matches `1.0.0`, `1.0.5`, but **not** `1.1.0`.
- `cthangover_game^1` matches `1.0.0`, `1.1.0`, `1.9.0`, but **not** `2.0.0`.

This follows the convention used by npm, Cargo, pip, Factorio, and Minecraft Fabric.

#### `conflicts_with`

Mods can declare mutual incompatibility:

```jsonc
{
  "id": "ff_battle",                                       // Canonical mod identifier
  "conflicts_with": ["card_battle"]                        // Declared incompatible mod IDs — informational only
}
```

!!! warning "Declarative metadata only"
    `conflicts_with` is parsed from the manifest and copied into catalog
    exports, but it is **never enforced at runtime**. Declaring a conflict
    disables nothing, blocks nothing, and shows no UI warning — two mods
    that declare each other as conflicting will still load and run
    together. Treat this field purely as informational metadata (e.g. for
    catalogs and mod platforms); do not rely on it to prevent broken
    combinations.

### Layer 2: Manual order — user preference

The manual order is a saved per-device preference in `user://mod_load_order.json` — it does **not**
transfer between devices or affect other players. Within the same dependency level, mods earlier
in the saved list load earlier, so a later-loaded mod still overrides them under the "last wins"
rule below.

The ordering machinery exists in the kernel (`ModLoadOrderStore` with `MoveUp`/`MoveDown`/`Remove`
helpers, `ModRegistry.InvalidateOrder` to recompute the cached order), but the current
**Mods & Collections** screen does not expose reorder buttons — the row order it displays is the
computed order. A mod whose dependency is missing is dropped from the registry entirely rather
than reordered, so the saved list can never place a dependent above its dependency.

### Layer 3: Alphabetical — deterministic fallback

When two mods have no dependency relationship and no manual order preference,
they are sorted alphabetically by their mod ID. This guarantees the same result
on every device and operating system.

## Conflict resolution strategy

The game uses **"last wins"** — the mod later in the load order overrides earlier mods:

| Asset type | Resolution |
|---|---|
| **Textures** | Same-name texture from a later mod overrides the earlier one |
| **JSON data** (characters, items, recipes, quests, interactives) | Same `ID` entry from a later mod overrides the earlier one |
| **Files** (audio, prefabs, etc.) | Same-path file from a later mod overrides the earlier one |
| **Shaders** | Same-name shader from a later mod overrides the earlier one |
| **Locale strings** | Same key from a later translation file overrides the earlier one |
| **Scene definitions** | Multiple mods can contribute to the same scene — they accumulate |
| **Scenarios** | Different names accumulate and run by their own `priority` and `after` metadata; the **same file name** from a later mod replaces the earlier scenario (even if it targets another scene) |
| **Property arrays** | Arrays in `ExtraProperties` (e.g. `Skills: ["a"]`) are **concatenated** across patches, not overwritten |

### Add or replace — the mod decides

Overriding is a right of a mod, not a duty. Nothing is replaced unless a mod
asks for it, and it asks in one way only:

- **To add**, use your own name: a new scenario for someone else's scene, a new
  item, a new background. It works next to the existing content.
- **To replace**, ship the **same name** (texture, file path, entity `ID`,
  scenario file name) **and declare `depends` on the mod that owns it**. The
  dependency puts your mod later in the load order, so your version wins and
  the original files stay untouched.
- **Same name without a dependency is a mistake**: two unrelated mods have no
  defined order between them, so the winner would be arbitrary. The
  resource atlas (the `resource_atlas` mod, `--resource-atlas`) reports it as an error
  ("one key provided by mods not linked by depends").

When a resource is overridden, a `Warning` is logged so you can inspect the conflict.
Overrides are also recorded in `ModConflictRegistry` (resource type, winning and overridden
mod id, whether arrays were concatenated); the registry is queryable from code and tests,
but the current Mods & Collections screen does not render a conflicts list.

## Version validation

On load, the game validates all dependency versions:

- If a required dependency is missing or its version is too low/too high → the mod is disabled with an error.
- `conflicts_with` is **not validated** — see the warning above; declared conflicts never disable anything.
- Disable reasons are **accumulated** (not overwritten) and shown in the UI status label.
- Cascade: if mod A is disabled, all mods depending on A are also disabled.

## UI overview

The **Mods** tab of the Mods & Collections screen shows one card per discovered mod:

- **Mod name, author, version, category and description**
- **Enable/disable toggle** — disable cascades to dependents; enabling re-enables the dependencies it needs
- **Status** — installed / update available / not installed / modified locally when a catalog entry exists
- **Delete** — offered only for mods whose files live in a writable mods root (`user://mods`);
  takes effect after a restart, like every other mod-set change

Mods are displayed in the computed load order (dependencies first). There is currently
no UI to reorder mods manually — see Layer 2 above.

## Storage

Manual load order is saved to `user://mod_load_order.json` as a JSON array of mod IDs:

```jsonc
["cthangover_game", "cooking", "hunting", "ui_overhaul"]              // Mod IDs in manual load order (first = highest priority)
```

New mods (not yet in the saved order) are placed alphabetically within their
dependency level — no manual position is assigned until the user explicitly reorders them.

Removed mods are cleaned up automatically on the next game launch.

## For mod creators

Declare dependencies with version constraints for safety:

```jsonc
{
  "id": "my_mod",                                          // Canonical mod identifier
  "depends": ["cthangover_game>=1", "ui_base>=1"],                  // Versioned dependency strings
  "version": "1.0.0"                                       // Semver version of this mod
}
```

- Use `>=1` (not `>=1.0.0`) — trailing zeros are unnecessary due to zero-padding comparison.
- Declare `conflicts_with` for documentation/catalog purposes if your mod is incompatible with another — remember it is informational only and not enforced.
- Use `?` for optional soft dependencies.

===== DOC docs/mods/locale/index.md: Locale — Localization =====
All in-game text lives in `.properties` files, organized by mod. The engine resolves locale keys at runtime based on the player's selected language.

## Directory structure

```filestree
locale/
├── en.properties               # UI text, English
├── ru.properties               # UI text, Russian
├── scenarios_en.properties     # Scenario dialogue, English
└── scenarios_ru.properties     # Scenario dialogue, Russian
```

- `{lang}.properties` — general UI text (menus, settings, item names, character names)
- `scenarios_{lang}.properties` — scenario dialogue lines referenced by `key=` parameters

Files are matched by locale code: an exact `{lang}.properties` name or any file
ending with `_{lang}.properties`. Locale files from **all** loaded mods are
merged into one translation, in mod load order — later mods override keys
defined by earlier ones.

## File format

Standard Java `.properties` format:

```properties
# Comment lines start with #
key = value
settings/language = Language
```
