1. Characters are defeated and a system publishes `CharactersDefeatedEvent` (e.g. `turn_battle_base`'s victory screen); the event also pulls the final battle health of every squad recruit back through `RecruitPartyBridge.SyncFromCard`;
2. `DefeatedCharactersRecruiter` resolves each id through `CharacterFactory` and skips templates with `RecruitmentChance <= 0` and enemies that are already captured (one recruit per enemy type);
3. The condition gate must pass: `RecruitBehaviourRegistry.CanRecruit` (all `IRecruitCondition`s, including the built-in `EnslaveCondition`);
4. The engine rolls 1-100 against the enemy's `RecruitmentChance`;
5. On success the enemy is registered via `RecruitingData.Instance.Add`;
6. In the Recruits panel the player presses "Add to party": `RecruitPartyBridge.AddToSquad` creates the placeholder `CharacterInfoData` entry and adds the template ID to `BattleSet`;
7. Character joins the battle set and becomes controllable; the recruit's health is pushed onto the card, and the character actions panel exposes the standard three action slots.

## Save state

`RecruitingSaveable` implements `IModSaveable` and stores the roster in the mod's own slot (mod id `recruiting`) as a `Recruits` `DataBlob`. `ModSaveableRegistry` discovers it through `ModAssemblyLoader` automatically. The core save (`__core__` slot) no longer contains a `Recruits` blob, and backward compatibility with old saves is not provided — old saves start with an empty roster.

On load the roster is replaced wholesale without re-firing `ConfigureRecruit`: persisted per-recruit properties (for example the nutrition timer) are restored as-is, so behaviours continue their tick logic from the saved state.

## Scenario integration

Use `party.add` (mod `characters_base`) to recruit directly from a scenario:

```scenario
action party.add id=Murakami
```

Show a notification without roster changes:

```scenario
action character.send_notification type=Murakami
```

## Relationship to skills

Recruited characters can acquire skills. `SkillData` is not a singleton - it is a plain instance owned by `RuntimeData`, accessible as `GameData.Instance.Runtime.SkillData`. It holds a single global set of player-owned skill IDs (lowercase strings such as `"slave_master"`) and is persisted with the save. Skills are not tied to individual recruits - they are a global unlock pool.

===== DOC docs/mods/rpg/events.md: Map-owned events =====
An invisible interaction area belongs directly to an RPG map's `Events` list. It needs no picture, `rpg_object` or `rpg_placement`. Use a placement for a visible chest or person, and a map event for an invisible doorway, story zone or action-key trigger.

```json
{
  "Id": "greeting", "X": 1, "Y": 1, "Width": 2, "Height": 1,
  "Trigger": "Step", "When": "self.done != 1", "Solid": false,
  "Commands": ["text \"Welcome to the meadow.\"", "flag self.done 1", "end"]
}
```

This is one entry inside `"Events": [...]` of an `rpg_map` entity. `Width` and `Height` default to 1; `Trigger` defaults to `Use` (E/F); `Solid` defaults to false. `When` is evaluated in the event's own scope. An optional `Name` is a locale key for the interaction cue. A shared `Scenario` can run before `Commands`; a `Teleport` is an alternative to both.

The event `greeting` on `my_meadow` has the scope `my_meadow/greeting`, so `flag self.done 1` writes `my_meadow/greeting:done`. IDs must be unique across both the map's events and placements. Preserve this identity when moving a former invisible placement into `Events`, so existing flags keep their meaning.

## Authoring with the mouse

In RPG Map Editor choose **Event**, drag the rectangle, choose **When stepped on** or **On use (E / F)**, type the commands, press **Apply event**, then **Save**. Optional settings expose the condition, shared scenario and teleport. Select moves the rectangle; its corner handles resize it in either projection. Undo restores a whole drag as one step.

These events save `X`, `Y`, `Width`, `Height` in the map. Legacy picture-less placements still use `Footprint.Size`; they remain supported but are a different storage format. Collision painting shows both, and `o`/`#` overrides their solidity.

## Extending someone else's map

Declare a dependency on the map's owner, then patch the map in `patches/rpg_map/<your_mod>.json`:

```json
{
  "Items": [{
    "Id": "my_meadow",
    "Events$add": [{
      "Id": "my_mod_exit", "X": 3, "Y": 1,
      "Trigger": "Step", "Teleport": {"Map": "rpg_village", "X": 1, "Y": 8, "Dir": "Right"}
    }]
  }]
}
```

`Events` declares `Id` as its merge key. `$add` therefore updates a matching event or appends a new one; `"Events$remove": ["my_mod_exit"]` removes by identity. It is a map patch, not a separate `rpg_map_event` entity kind. The editor, validator and resource atlas scan event conditions, scripts, shared scenarios and teleport targets.

Implementation: `RpgMap.Events`, `RpgMapEvent`, the RPG interaction runner, and `mods/rpg_editor/src/Document/RpgMapDocument.Events.cs`.

===== DOC docs/mods/rpg/index.md: RPG maps, objects and events =====
`rpg` supplies a grid world over `shell2d_base`. A map is a logical scene: ordinary dialogs, scene transitions, music and saves still work. WASD or arrows walk, Shift runs, and E/F uses the object or event the player faces. Party members follow the leader's trail and gather after a teleport. The leader's `Charset` supplies the walking picture.

This is model v2. Pictures are individual PNGs in `rpg_images/`; maps do not use a tileset atlas. The separate world-map travel overlay is described in Map & Travel Zones.

## A minimal map

Put this entity in `maps/meadow.json` in your own mod. Declare `rpg>=1`, a tile renderer and `village_pack>=1` in `depends` for this example.

```json
{
  "Kind": "rpg_map",
  "Items": [{
    "Id": "my_meadow", "Name": "my_game/meadow/name",
    "Width": 4, "Height": 3,
    "Legend": {"g": "village_pack/ground/grass"},
    "Ground": ["gggg", "gggg", "gggg"],
    "Collision": ["....", "....", "...."],
    "Spawn": {"X": 1, "Y": 1, "Dir": "Down"}
  }]
}
```

Also supply a scene definition with `"name": "my_meadow"`, following Scenes, and select this scene as the collection entry or enter it with `switch_scene my_meadow`. Map IDs contain no `/`.

| Map field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Width`, `Height` | Cell dimensions |
| `TileSize`, `PatchSize` | Picture pixels per cell (default 32) and surface material patch size (128) |
| `Zoom` | View zoom, default 2 |
| `Legend` | One character to an `rpg_object` ID; `.` is empty |
| `Ground`, `Structure` | Rows of surface and segment legend codes respectively |
| `Collision` | `.` uses object collision, `#` blocks, `o` forces passage even through a solid object |
| `Strokes` | Ordered cell edits over the base rows; entries merge by `Id` |
| `Spawn` | Default `X`, `Y`, `Dir` |
| `Variant` | Default object variant for this map |
| `Ambient`, `LightMode`, `Lights` | Fixed ambient colour, `cycle`/`day`/`night`, and map lights (radius in cells) |
| `Atmosphere`, `Effects` | Whole-map profile and additional profiles or painted cell masks; see Effects |
| `Events` | Map-owned interaction rectangles; see Map events |
| `Encounters` | Read by the optional battles integration, rather than by the grid world |

## Objects and placements

An object is an entity of kind `rpg_object`, normally in `objects/`. A placement is an entity of kind `rpg_placement`, normally in `placements/<map>/`. Its ID is `<map>/<local_id>`.

| `Form` | Use |
|---|---|
| `surface` | Ground material through `Surface` |
| `segment` | Joined wall or fence cells through `Segment` |
| `sprite` | One or more pictures in `Parts`, with a multi-cell `Footprint` |
| `actor` | A 1×1 character using `Charset`, `Facing`, `Column` and an optional `Route` |

```json
{
  "Kind": "rpg_placement",
  "Items": [{
    "Id": "my_meadow/chest",
    "Object": "village_pack/chest", "X": 2, "Y": 1,
    "Interact": {"Trigger": "Use", "Commands": [
      "text \"There is a note inside.\"", "flag self.opened 1", "end"
    ]}
  }]
}
```

`Solid` overrides the form default (surfaces are walkable; other forms are solid). `Footprint.Size` is `[width,height]`; optional `Footprint.Solid` rows use `#` for blocked cells. `Hidden` removes drawing, collision and interaction. Explicit map collision codes take precedence.

`Parts` entries can have `Id`, `Image` (relative to `rpg_images/`, without extension), pixel `Crop`, cell `At` and `Size`, `Geometry`, projection restriction `Only`, `DrawOrder` and `SortOffset`. Geometry supports upright pictures, ground pictures, planes and explicit quads. `Anchor` is the object's sorting and standing point in cells. The object editor includes a visual parts workspace for movement, resizing and depth checks with a reference character.

Objects support `Extends` and named `Variants`. A placement's overrides merge over the object and its selected variant. Ordered `States` choose the last matching `When`; supplied state components replace their corresponding component. For example, a state can hide a defeated enemy or remove a locked door's teleport. `self.opened` belongs to this placement, not every copy of the chest: it resolves to the story flag `my_meadow/chest:opened`.

`Interact` accepts `Scenario` and/or inline `Commands` (scenario first, commands afterwards), or a standalone `Teleport` with `Map`, `X`, `Y`, `Dir`. This allows a shared character dialog followed by `party.add`. An `end` in the shared scenario does not discard the appended interaction commands.

## Battles and teleporting

`rpg` has no dependency on `turn_battle_base`. When both are loaded, the integration `rpg__battles` supplies random encounters and `rpg.battle`:

```scenario
action rpg.battle enemies=rpg_wolf,rpg_slime win_flag=my_wolf_dead
end
```

Victory sets the named flag to 1 and returns to the saved map cell. Random battles use map data such as `"Encounters": {"Steps": 48, "Groups": ["rpg_slime", "rpg_slime,rpg_slime"]}`; 0 steps disables them. No battle mod means no random battles. Teleports belong to `rpg` itself:

```scenario
action rpg.teleport map=rpg_village x=1 y=8 dir=right
end
```

## Projection, editing and saving

`tile_render_base` consumes the prepared world. `tile_topdown_base` draws squares from above; `tile_isometric_base` draws 2:1 diamonds. Set the collection setting `tile.renderer` to `topdown` or `isometric`, or override it with `--tile-renderer=isometric`. This changes presentation, not map coordinates or event identities.

The RPG Map Editor paints grids, collision and effect masks; places objects and characters; edits interactions and map events; supports rectangle selection, copy/paste, undo/redo, spawn and teleport targets; and can play from the chosen cell. The RPG Object Editor edits the reusable definitions, parts, states, variants and PNG imports. Both use the game's renderer. Changes to a dependency are saved as patches in the chosen writable mod.

The `rpg` save slot owns `Position`; story flags, including `self.` values, are in the core `Flags` blob. Keep placement and event IDs stable across updates. See Save data and Developer tools.

Implementation: `mods/rpg/src/Model/`, `mods/rpg/src/Play/`, `mods/rpg/src/State/`, `mods/rpg/integrations/battles/`, and the three `tile_*_base` mods.

===== DOC docs/mods/save/api.md: Mod save API =====
Implement a public `IModSaveable` class in normal mod sources. The assembly consumer discovers it and registers the handler under its mod ID. Use one handler per mod and version each logical `DataBlob` independently.

## Handler contract

```csharp
namespace ExampleMod
{
    using Cthangover.Core.Mods;
    using Cthangover.Core.Settings;

    public class CounterSave : IModSaveable
    {
        public static int Visits { get; set; }

        public void OnSave(ModSlot slot)
        {
            // ModId, ModName and ModVersion are already populated.
            slot.Data["Visits"] = new DataBlob(1, Visits);
        }

        public bool OnLoad(ModSlot slot, out string errorMessage)
        {
            errorMessage = null;
            if (slot?.Data == null || !slot.Data.TryGetValue("Visits", out var blob))
                return true;
            if (blob.Version != 1)
            {
                errorMessage = "Unsupported Visits blob version";
                return false;
            }
            Visits = blob.ReadJson<int>();
            return true;
        }

        public void OnJoinedExistingGame()
        {
            // Optional: supply starter state when no slot was restored.
        }
    }
}
```

Reset new-game state through the normal reset lifecycle as well; loading is not the only way a game starts. `OnJoinedExistingGame` is called for new, dormant or rejected mechanics with no restored slot. Do not use it to overwrite a successfully restored value.

The preferred load method is `bool OnLoad(ModSlot, out string)`. The legacy `void OnLoadVoid(ModSlot)` may throw; the default implementation delegates to it. Override at least one or the default throws `NotSupportedException`. `OnJoinedExistingGame()` has a no-op default. There is no `IsRequired` metadata property.

## Blobs and migrations

Use `slot.Data.TryGetValue` for missing keys and inspect `blob.Version` before reading a payload. For a schema upgrade, keep branches reading old DTOs and convert them to the current DTO; write only the current version. Return false with a descriptive error if conversion is impossible.

- `new DataBlob(2, data)` stores a versioned JSON payload, read with `ReadJson<T>()`.
- Key-value fields use `SetInt`/`GetInt`, `SetFloat`/`GetFloat`, `SetString`/`GetString`, `SetBool`/`GetBool`.
- Raw bytes use `new DataBlob(bytes)` or `SetBytes`/`GetBytes`.

Choose one access mode per blob. Key-value writes and whole JSON writes replace one another. The core fills `ModId`, `ModName`, `ModVersion`; a handler may set `MinCoreVersion`, `CustomData` and its own data. A mod slot requiring a newer core is kept dormant instead of handed to the old implementation.

## Discovery and error handling

`ModAssemblyLoader.RegisterAssembly(assembly, modId)` fans out through `ModSaveableConsumer` and `ModSaveableRegistry`.

| Registry operation | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `RegisterAssembly(assembly, modId)` | Discover save handlers |
| `CollectSaveData()` | Create metadata and call each handler's `OnSave` |
| `DispatchLoad(modData, failedModIds)` | Restore compatible live slots; collect failures |
| `NotifyJoined(restoredModIds)` | Notify handlers without a restored slot |

An `OnSave` exception is logged and that handler's new slot is skipped. A false/throwing `OnLoad` is reported and its original slot is retained as dormant (`LoadFailed`). Other compatible mod slots continue loading. The core does not inspect the payload or migrate it on the handler's behalf. Do not depend on the order of unrelated save handlers.

## Save browser API

| `SaveService` member | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `SaveDirectory`, `Path` | Active game's Godot directory and globalized path |
| `Save(fileName)`, `Load(fileName)` | Save/load the active game's logical slot |
| `ApplyPackage(package, sourceName)` | Restore an already read package |
| `GameDirectory(gameId)` | Directory for a specific game |
| `ListSaves(gameId)` | Unlimited saved-slot list with previews |
| `GetSaveSlots(slotCount)` | Compatibility grid with empty slot placeholders |
| `ListGamesWithSaves()` | Games represented in the save browser |
| `NextSlotName(gameId)` | Next available logical name |
| `ReadPackage(gameId, fileName)` | Read without applying |
| `BackupSave(gameId, fileName)` | Create a backup |
| `CopyGameSaves(fromGameId, toGameId)` | Copy saves for a duplicated game and rewrite the game ID |
| `LastLoadDormancy` | Classification from the last applied save |
| `PurgeDormantData()` | Explicitly remove dormant slots from saves and current session |
| `DeleteSave(fileName)` | Delete active-game JSON and PNG |

Named-game saves live at `user://saves/<game_id>/`; unscoped development saves use `user://saves/`. Slot names are sanitized for filesystem use.

## Preview and screenshots

`RuntimeSerializer.ReadSlotPreview(metadata)` reads `CurrentSceneName`, `GameTime`, `CharacterCount` from the reserved slot's `CustomData` without reading gameplay blobs. `SaveSlotInfo` also exposes game identity and the saved mod composition; see Data model.

`SaveScreenshotService.CaptureAndSave(fileName)` captures the background view and writes a 320×180 PNG beside the JSON. If no background is available it uses a solid fallback image before scaling. `LoadScreenshot(fileName)` uses the active game's directory; `LoadScreenshotFromPath(path)` can load a preview from another game's directory. Both return null if the PNG is absent or cannot be decoded. Screenshots are separate files, not package blobs.

===== DOC docs/mods/save/data-model.md: Save Data Model =====
## Save file structure (SavePackage)

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SaveId` | `string` | User-facing save name (e.g. "slot_1") |
| `Timestamp` | `DateTime` | UTC timestamp when the save was created |
| `CoreVersion` | `string` | Version of the game core that created this save |
| `ModData` | `Dict<string, ModSlot>` | Core and active mod data slots |
| `Game` | `SaveGameInfo` | Game ID/name/version and ordered `Mods` (`Id`, `Version`) snapshot; null for unscoped development saves |
| `Dormant` | `Dict<string, ModSlot>` | Inactive/incompatible slots preserved for a later return |

## `"__core__"` slot structure

The core slot uses these blob keys:

| Blob key | Content type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Time` | `long` | In-game clock tick (minutes elapsed) |
| `Lamp` | key-value | `Radius` (float), `Influence` (float) |
| `ActionPool` | `List<string>` | Persistent action card pool IDs |
| `Skills` | `List<string>` | Owned skill IDs |
| `CompletedScenarios` | `List<string>` | Completed scene-chain scenario IDs |
| `MapZones` | `MapZoneStateData` | World-map open/visible overrides against authored defaults |
| `Flags` | `Dictionary<string,string>` | Global flags and scoped `<map>/<event>:<name>` flags |

## Mod-owned slots

| Slot | Blob keys | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| `characters_base` | `Characters`, `BattleSet` | Party snapshots and active combat-party IDs |
| `items_base` | `Inventory`, `Recipes` | Item id/count pairs (including unresolved entries) and learned recipes |
| `quests_base` | `Quests` | Quest state, including unresolved records |
| `rpg` | `Position` | RPG map and position state |
| `stats_base` | `Stats` | Stats sheets and progression |
| `equipment` | `Worn` | Equipped item state |
| `recruiting` | `Recruits` | Recruit roster |
| `npc_barks_base` | `History` | Completed ambient definitions and remaining cooldowns |

These slots are written by the owning mods' `IModSaveable` implementations; they are not part of `__core__`. Current loading does not migrate former core-owned character/item/quest blobs automatically. A currently running battle, dialog execution pointer, speech bubble or transient speech event is not a saved snapshot.

Slot preview metadata (`CurrentSceneName`, `GameTime`, `CharacterCount`) is stored in `ModMetadata.CustomData` as a JSON object, so the slot list UI can read it without deserialising game-state blobs.

Subsystems **not persisted** by `BuildCoreSlot` today: there are no blob keys for the active battle encounter (held by the turn-battle mod in `BattleRequest.Current`), so an encounter in progress resets between sessions. The recruit roster is not a core blob either: it is persisted by the `recruiting` mod in its own `ModSlot` through `IModSaveable`.

The saved `CurrentSceneName` is used for the slot-list UI and scene navigation after loading; `RuntimeData.CurrentScene` itself is not stored — it is a read-only property resolved live from the `SceneManager`/current scene at runtime.

## ModSlot

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Metadata` | `ModMetadata` | Structured metadata for core validation |
| `Data` | `Dict<string, DataBlob>` | Named data blocks defined by the mod |

## ModMetadata

The core pre-fills `ModId`, `ModName`, and `ModVersion` from `IModInfo` before calling `OnSave`. The mod only needs to set `MinCoreVersion`, `CustomData`, and the data blobs.

| Field | Type | Set by | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ModId` | `string` | core | Mod ID (from `IModInfo.Id`) |
| `ModName` | `string` | core | Human-readable name (from `IModInfo.DisplayTitle`) |
| `ModVersion` | `string` | core | Version of the installed mod (from `IModInfo.Version`, read from `manifest.json`) |
| `MinCoreVersion` | `string` | mod | Minimum core version required |
| `Dormant` | `bool` | core | True while the slot is retained inactive |
| `DormantReason` | `string` | core | `NotLoaded`, `Downgrade`, `CoreTooOld`, or `LoadFailed` |
| `DormantSince` | `DateTime?` | core | First dormancy date |
| `CustomData` | `string` | mod | Free-form JSON for extra metadata |

## DataBlob

A versioned byte container with three access modes:

| Mode | Methods | Use case |
|---|---|---|
| **Key-value** | `GetInt/SetInt`, `GetFloat/SetFloat`, `GetString/SetString`, `GetBool/SetBool` | Simple fields like HP, mana, lamp radius |
| **JSON** | `ReadJson<T>()`, `WriteJson<T>(data)` | Complex objects with versioned schemas |
| **Binary** | `GetBytes()`, `SetBytes(byte[])` | Raw data (textures, compressed payloads) |

Key-value and JSON modes conflict — calling `WriteJson` overwrites the internal dictionary, and calling `SetInt` after `WriteJson` replaces the JSON object with a dictionary. Choose one mode per blob.

### DataBlob constructors

```csharp
new DataBlob();                          // version=1, empty
new DataBlob(version: 2);               // version=2, empty
new DataBlob(version: 2, myObject);      // version=2, serialises myObject as JSON byte payload
new DataBlob(rawBytes);                  // version=1, stores raw bytes
```

The raw payload is also exposed directly via the public `Payload` property (`byte[]`; assigning `null` is coerced to an empty array). Unlike `GetBytes()`, reading `Payload` does **not** flush pending key-value entries into the byte array first.

## SaveSlotInfo

Returned by `SaveService.GetSaveSlots(slotCount)` for the load-game UI. There is no numeric slot ID/index field — the logical slot identity is `FileName` (e.g. `slot_1`).

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `FileName` | `string` | Logical slot name (e.g. `slot_1`), used as the save-file identifier |
| `SaveTime` | `DateTime` | UTC timestamp from `SavePackage.Timestamp` (`DateTime.MinValue` for empty slots) |
| `SceneName` | `string` | Scene at save time (from the core slot preview; `"unknown"` fallback) |
| `ScreenshotPath` | `string` | Godot path of the thumbnail PNG (e.g. `user://saves/<game_id>/slot_1.png`) |
| `HasScreenshot` | `bool` | `true` if a thumbnail PNG exists on disk |
| `IsEmpty` | `bool` | `true` when no JSON save file exists for this slot |
| `GameTime` | `long` | In-game time tick at save moment |
| `CharacterCount` | `int` | Number of characters at save moment |
| `GameId`, `GameName` | `string` | Game identity and saved display name |
| `Mods` | `List<SaveGameMod>` | Saved mod-composition snapshot |
| `LampPercent` | `float` | **Known placeholder**: currently always written as `0f` by `GetSaveSlots` — no real lamp data is computed yet |

===== DOC docs/mods/save/index.md: Save system =====
A `SavePackage` contains a reserved `__core__` slot, mod-owned slots in `ModData`, the game composition snapshot in `Game`, and preserved inactive slots in `Dormant`. The kernel routes blobs to their owners; it does not interpret their payloads.

## Where saves live

A game's saves and PNG thumbnails live in `user://saves/<game_id>/`. The save browser supports an unlimited list; `slot_1`, `slot_2`, … are logical names, not a hard slot limit. Development runs without a game retain the unscoped saves directory. `Game` records the collection's ID, name, version and mod versions at save time; this snapshot does not force the old composition to be reinstalled.

## Save and load

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    S[Save] --> C[Build core slot]
    S --> M[Collect mod slots]
    C --> P[Write package with Game and Dormant]
    M --> P
    P --> L[Load and validate core slot]
    L --> D[Classify mod slots]
    D --> R[Restore compatible live slots]
    D --> K[Carry incompatible slots as Dormant]
    R --> J[Notify mods joining an existing game]
```

Loading first requires a valid core slot. It then classifies each mod slot against the **loaded** mod set and versions, restores core state, and dispatches compatible mod data. A slot whose mod is missing, downgraded, requires a newer core, or rejects its payload is retained as dormant; these mod-local problems do not abort the entire load. The reasons are `NotLoaded`, `Downgrade`, `CoreTooOld`, `LoadFailed`.

Dormant data is written forward unchanged and can revive when the compatible mod returns. New mechanics without restored data start from new-game defaults and receive `IModSaveable.OnJoinedExistingGame()`. The save settings provide explicit dormant-data cleanup (`SaveService.PurgeDormantData()`); data is otherwise retained. Unresolved item and quest records are also retained by their owners rather than silently lost.

## State ownership

The core currently keeps time, lamp, skills/action pool, completed scenarios, world-map overrides and story flags. Characters, inventory, recipes, quests, RPG position, stats, equipment, recruits and NPC background history each have their own mod slots. There is no active-battle snapshot or automatic saving of concurrent speech execution pointers.

Removing a mechanic from a game's collection therefore preserves its save slot for a later return. This does not convert old saves from before a subsystem moved out of `__core__`: see Versioning.

## Mod contract

Implement `IModSaveable`; discovery supplies one slot per mod. The core fills `ModId`, `ModName`, `ModVersion`; the mod sets `MinCoreVersion`, optional `CustomData`, and `Data`. There is no `IsRequired` field in the current metadata DTO. Use versioned blobs, handle missing keys, and keep another mod's state out of your own payload.

`__core__` remains reserved; a mod cannot replace it. Scene navigation after loading uses the core preview's `CurrentSceneName`, while mod state restores before gameplay resumes.

- Data model — package, slot ownership, blobs and preview fields.
- Mod API — save handlers, migrations and save-browser methods.
- Versioning and compatibility — version comparison and dormancy.

===== DOC docs/mods/save/versioning.md: Versioning & Compatibility =====
## Backward compatibility

Old saves (without `ModData`, `SaveId`, `Timestamp`, `CoreVersion` fields) will fail to load because the `"__core__"` slot is required. The old `SaveData` format must be migrated manually or via a one-time converter tool.

## Version comparison

During slot classification the core compares `ModMetadata.ModVersion` from the save with `IModInfo.Version` from the installed mod. Both values originate from the `"version"` field in `manifest.json`.

### Comparison strategy

The core uses **per-segment comparison** via `VersionHelper.Compare` (Core/Utils/VersionHelper.cs). There is no `System.Version` parsing anywhere — the algorithm is:

1. Both strings are trimmed and split on `.`. The shorter segment array is padded with `"0"` to equal length (`"1.0"` equals `"1.0.0"`).
2. Segments are compared **pairwise, left to right**. For each position: if *both* segments parse as `int`, they are compared numerically (handles `"9" < "10"`); otherwise that segment pair is compared with an ordinal string comparison (`String.CompareOrdinal`).
3. The first non-zero comparison decides the result.

Consequences: a segment like `"1b"` does **not** parse as int, so it compares **ordinally**, not numerically. Mixed versions are fine — each segment independently picks numeric or ordinal comparison.

### Supported version formats

| Format | Example | Comparison |
|---|---|---|
| Numeric segments | `1.0.0`, `2.5.3.1`, `1.0` | Numeric per segment; shorter string padded with `"0"` |
| Letter in a segment | `1b.2.0` | That segment compares ordinally (`"1b"` vs `"10"` is ordinal, not numeric) |
| Prefixed tags | `v1`, `v2.0` | Ordinal per segment (`"v2" > "v10"` ordinally) |
| Codename-style | `Enigma1.5`, `Enigma2.0` | Ordinal per segment |
| Alpha/beta tags | `alpha-1`, `beta-3` | Ordinal per segment |

### Validation rules

| Condition | Result |
|---|---|
| No version in save or installed mod | Skip check |
| `save.Version > installed.Version` | Keep this slot **dormant** (`Downgrade`); compatible parts of the game still load |
| `save.Version < installed.Version` | **Warning** — save is from an older mod version; the mod must handle migration in `OnLoad` |
| `save.Version == installed.Version` | OK |

## Adding version to manifest.json

Every mod's `manifest.json` must include a semver version string:

```jsonc
{
  "name": "My Mod",                                        // Human-readable mod name
  "version": "1.0.0",                                      // Semver version — used for save validation and migration detection
  "author": "...",                                         // Author credit
  "description": "...",                                    // Short description
  "sources": ["src/**/*.cs"]                              // Glob patterns for C# source files
}
```

This value flows through `ModManifest.Version` → `IModInfo.Version` → `ModMetadata.ModVersion` and is used for the save-system version checks above.

## Important rules for mod authors

- **Add `"version"` to `manifest.json`** — the core uses it for save validation and migration detection.
- **One `IModSaveable` per mod** — create a single implementation in your assembly.
- **Never set `ModId`, `ModName`, or `ModVersion`** — the core pre-fills them. Set `MinCoreVersion`, `CustomData`, and `Data`.
- **Prefer `bool OnLoad(ModSlot, out string)`** — explicit contract, no exceptions. Override `OnLoadVoid` only for legacy/compatibility.
- **Mods must not depend on each other's save data** — the dispatch order is undefined.
- **Version your `DataBlob` blocks** — use separate blobs per logical entity, version each independently.
- **Handle missing keys** — use `TryGetValue` and provide defaults.

## Missing mods and rejoining

A missing/unloaded mod or a mod whose slot requires a newer core is kept dormant (`NotLoaded` or `CoreTooOld`). A rejected `OnLoad` becomes `LoadFailed`. These mod-local outcomes do not abort the whole package. A missing or incompatible reserved core slot still blocks load. See Save system.

When a compatible mod returns, its dormant data can revive. A new mechanic without restored data receives `OnJoinedExistingGame()` after defaults are prepared. Retain a stable mod ID; renaming it does not automatically transfer the old slot. Cleanup in the save settings explicitly purges dormant data.

The current format does not migrate the former `Characters`/`BattleSet`, `Inventory`/`Recipes`, or `Quests` blobs out of `__core__`; those owners now read their own mod slots. Older saves may therefore start those subsystems at fresh defaults unless a dedicated converter is supplied.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/index.md: Scenes =====
Scene definitions map scene IDs to default backgrounds, ambient music, and entry-point scenarios. Scenes are the navigation graph of the game — the player moves between them.

## JSON structure

```jsonc
{
  "name": "scene_id",                                       // Unique scene identifier (referenced by switch_scene)
  "defaultBackground": "path/to/background",                // Path to background image loaded on scene enter (or array of paths)
  "defaultAmbient": "locations/wind",                       // Looping ambient sound ID; empty stops ambient
  "defaultScenario": "scenarios/path/to/default.scenario"   // Fallback when no scene events are registered
}
```

## Field reference

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `name` | Unique scene identifier (e.g. `home_kitchen`, `town_entry`). Referenced by `switch_scene` in DSL |
| `defaultBackground` | Path to the background image loaded on scene enter (relative to `backgrounds/`). Can be a single string or an array; entering the scene chooses one array entry randomly |
| `defaultAmbient` | Ambient sound ID relative to `sounds/`; empty/null stops the ambient channel. Music playlists are selected separately by scene and route state |
| `defaultScenario` | Fallback scenario path relative to the mod root, used when no scene events are registered |

## Real examples

From `mods/cthangover_game/scenes/`:

```jsonc
{
  "name": "home_kitchen",                                 // Scene ID — used with switch_scene and ui[] entries
  "defaultBackground": "home/kitchen",                    // Background image path (relative to backgrounds/)
  "defaultAmbient": "",                                   // Stop ambient sounds for this scene
  "defaultScenario": "scenarios/home/home_kitchen_default.scenario"  // Entry-point scenario
}
```

```jsonc
{
  "name": "home_vestibule",                                   // Scene ID — vestibule/entrance of the player's home
  "defaultBackground": "home/vestibule",                      // Background image path (relative to backgrounds/)
  "defaultAmbient": "",                                       // Stop ambient sounds for this scene
  "defaultScenario": "scenarios/home/home_vestibule_default.scenario"  // Entry-point scenario
}
```

## Scene navigation

Scenes are connected through the `switch_scene` DSL command:

```scenario
switch_scene home_vestibule
end
```

And through `option` jumps that lead to `switch_scene`:

```scenario
select "[Where to?]" key=home_outside/prompt
option "Enter the house" key=home_outside/opt_enter -> :enter
option "To the town" key=home_outside/opt_town -> :town

:enter
switch_scene home_vestibule
end

:town
switch_scene town_entry
end
```

## Scene lifecycle

When the player enters a scene, the engine:

1. Collects the scene's events — `.scenario` files targeting the scene plus `[SceneEvent]` C# classes (see Scene Event Metadata) — and sorts them by `priority` with `after:` dependencies resolved
2. Publishes the `scene_entered` lifecycle event on the Event Bus; engine subscribers apply `defaultBackground` (subscriber priority 0), start `defaultAmbient` (5), and update the music type (10)
3. Evaluates every event's `condition:` and enqueues the passing scenarios into the scene's event chain, where they run one after another in enqueue order
4. If no events are registered at all, falls back to the scene's `defaultScenario`
5. When leaving, publishes `scene_exited`

## Hooking into scenes from mods

There is no manifest subscription list; mods extend a scene in one of three ways:

- **A `.scenario` file targeting the scene** — the most common hook. The Cooking mod adds a "Cook" option to the kitchen with a `home_kitchen` scenario at a lower `priority` than the core's default one.
- **A `[SceneEvent]` C# class** — declarative code hook compiled into the mod assembly; runs in the same event chain as scenarios (see Scene Event Metadata).
- **A manifest `ui` entry bound to the scene** — panels and overlays declared in `manifest.json` accept a `"scene": "home_kitchen"` field; the element is then only available in that scene and is hidden when the player leaves it.

## Scenario priority system

Multiple `.scenario` files can target the same `scene:`. The one with the **lowest** `priority` number runs first. If that scenario's `condition:` is unmet, the next-highest priority scenario is tried.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    A["home_kitchen_cooking.scenario<br/>priority: 5"] -->|runs first| S{{"Scene:<br/>home_kitchen"}}
    B["home_kitchen_default.scenario<br/>priority: 10"] -->|fallback| S
```

This allows mods to extend scenes non-destructively by writing additional scenario files with lower priority numbers.

## Adding a new scene

1. Create `scenes/{name}.json` with background, playlist, and default scenario
2. Create the background image(s) in `backgrounds/{name}/`
3. Write the default scenario in `scenarios/{path}.scenario`
4. Add `switch_scene {name}` commands to an existing scene to create a navigation path
5. Register a `[SceneEvent]` class, add extra `.scenario` files targeting the scene, or declare a `ui` entry with `"scene": "{name}"` in your manifest if needed

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/background.md: Background NPC scenarios =====
`npc_barks_base` hosts concurrent `.scenario` programs on RPG maps. Speech appears above NPCs while the player can keep walking. Each execution uses the shared `dialog_base` parser and its own `DialogRuntime`, variables, waits, roles and debugger. It does not occupy the modal dialog box. The mod is a prototype; its supported command subset is deliberately limited.

[image: Two concurrent NPC speech bubbles at the city gates in Junior Reaper & Paper Grief.]

## Defining a conversation

The game must include `npc_barks_base` and supply the actor placements named below. Put the file in `scenarios/ambient/greeting.scenario`:

```scenario
scene: my_meadow
channel: ambient
actor.guard: my_meadow/guard
anchor: guard
condition: near("guard", "player", 4)
cooldown: 30
once: false
priority: 0
---
:greet
action npc.look who=guard target=player
text "Good evening." who=guard duration=3 cue=guard_greeting
end
```

The definition ID is its path relative to `scenarios/` without the extension: `ambient/greeting`. This is distinct from the modal scene-chain scenario identity. Translate speech with ordinary `key=` entries.

| Header | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `channel: ambient` | Selects the background host; omitted channel means ordinary modal dialog |
| `scene` | Required RPG map ID |
| `actor.<role>` | Required owned participant; full placement ID must start with `<scene>/` |
| `anchor` | An owned role, defaulting to the first role; supplies the default speaker and `self.` scope |
| `condition` | Admission condition in this execution's spatial context |
| `cooldown` | Nonnegative finite seconds after completion, default 30 |
| `once` | Save completion history and stop admitting the definition after success, default false |
| `priority` | Lower first; equal priority sorts by definition ID |
| `on`, `cue`, `source_actor` | Optional reaction to a speech cue; see below |

Actors must exist, be visible and be stationary. Owned participants are reserved together: two conversations cannot own the same actor at once. Disjoint conversations can run concurrently (up to 32). Their actors' routes are held until completion or interruption. E/F interaction with a participant interrupts its bark and releases it for the player's dialog.

## Commands and spatial conditions

Supported commands are `text`, `ptext`, `when`, `goto`, `delay`, `flag`, `set`, `cset`, `end`, and **only** `action npc.look`. Modal choices, scene switches, battles, inventory actions and arbitrary `action` names are rejected during ambient compilation. `wait=false` on speech is rejected.

`who=` selects an owned speaker role; `duration=` is a positive finite reading time in seconds. Without a duration, the host estimates 2–8 seconds from text length. `cue=` emits speech events. `npc.look who=<owned_role> target=<role|player|source>` keeps that actor facing the target while the run owns it.

Conditions share the story condition grammar and add:

| Condition | Prototype meaning |
|---|---|
| `near("guard", "player", 4)` | Euclidean distance within an integer radius of 0–1000 logical cells |
| `visible("guard")` | Participant exists and is logically visible; not a camera or wall visibility test |
| `moving("player")` | Whether that resolved actor is currently moving |
| `can_hear("guard", "source")` | Distance within 8 cells; no wall or door acoustics |

`player` and `source` are reserved roles, not names for `actor.*` declarations. `source` resolves to the actor whose speech triggered a reaction.

`when` can wait for a live condition instead of branching once:

```scenario
when "!near(\"guard\", \"player\", 5)" wait=true timeout=8 else=still_here -> :left
:still_here
text "Still here?" who=guard
end
:left
text "There they go." who=guard
end
```

`timeout` must be positive and finite. The success arrow and `else` target are optional; a missing target continues to the next line. The modal host also supports this wait form.

## Responding to speech

A second definition can declare:

```scenario
scene: my_meadow
channel: ambient
actor.vendor: my_meadow/vendor
on: speech.finished
cue: guard_greeting
source_actor: my_meadow/guard
condition: can_hear("vendor", "source")
cooldown: 30
---
:reply
action npc.look who=vendor target=source
text "Evening!" who=vendor duration=2
end
```

`on` accepts `speech.started` or `speech.finished` and requires `cue`. `source_actor` optionally filters by exact placement ID. Reactions are deferred to the next world update; old-session events, duplicate events, events older than two simulation seconds and reaction depth beyond eight are discarded. An interruption emits `speech.interrupted`, not a false finish; this phase is not an accepted reaction trigger.

## Patching a labeled block

Place a patch under `patches/scenarios/` and depend on the definition's owner:

```scenario
patch_of: ambient/greeting
replace: greet
---
text "The market closes soon." who=guard duration=3
end
```

`replace` preserves the label and replaces its body up to the next label; it cannot introduce labels. `remove: greet` removes the labeled block. `append_blocks: true` appends explicitly labeled blocks, provided the original body ends with `end`. Patches address stable labels, never line numbers. Compilation rejects duplicate labels and missing jump targets. This patch mechanism belongs to the ambient host.

## Editing, debugging and persistence

Scenario Editor validates background buffers and can preview one scenario or its company of nearby scenarios. Save publishes source changes; existing runs keep their compiled program until restarted. Scenario Studio's **Background** tab lists definitions and their admission status separately from running executions. Select a run to pause, step, continue, advance its clock by a second, stop or restart it. Source IDs select breakpoints; run IDs select one execution.

[image: Scenario Studio's Background tab showing the catalog and a live NPC execution at the city gates.]

Menus and ordinary blocking tools pause the simulation and reading timers. The background authoring tools use observation mode; Studio also has an explicit whole-world pause. Scene exit, world replacement and applied mod reload abort transient runs and release their participants.

The `npc_barks_base` save slot stores a `History` blob: completed definition IDs and remaining cooldowns. Bubbles, execution pointers and queued speech events are not saved or replayed; active runs receive a small cooldown on save. An aborted run is not marked completed. Flags remain ordinary saved game flags.

Implementation: `mods/npc_barks_base/src/` and `mods/dialog_base/src/IScenarioExecutionHost.cs`. The authoring seam is `BackgroundScenarios.Provider` / `IBackgroundScenarioTools`. Tests: `npc_barks` and `npc_barks_tools` in the test manifest.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/expressions.md: Numeric expressions and dialog variables =====
`expressions_base` supplies the shared expression parser used by dialogs, schemas and stats. Arithmetic works in a visual novel without RPG or character stats.

```scenario
set money=rnd(3,5)
cset total "1+2+${money}"
ptext "You found ${money} coins."
action item.add id=money/coin count=${money}
end
```

The final action additionally requires `items_base` and content defining the item. The random value is calculated once and reused, including in translated `ptext` values and action parameters.

| Assignment | Result |
|---|---|
| `set name value` | Literal string |
| `set name=expression` | Calculated number |
| `cset name expression` or `cset name=expression` | Calculated number |
| `set name = value` | Invalid intended assignment: space-separated literal form stores `=` |

Quote expressions containing spaces. Names are case-sensitive. Variables belong to the current runtime; for persistent state read by conditions, use `flag`. Each concurrent background execution has its own variable dictionary.

## Grammar and errors

Numbers use a decimal point. Arithmetic: `+ - * / % ^`, parentheses and unary signs. Comparisons: `< <= > >= == !=`. Boolean results are 0/1; `! && ||` combine them. Both `condition ? yes : no` and `if(condition, yes, no)` select lazily. Multiplication precedes addition, powers associate right, and `-2^2` is -4.

Functions: `min`, `max`, `abs`, `floor`, `ceil`, `round`, `sqrt`, `clamp`, `rnd`, `rand`, `chance`. `rnd(a,b)` requires ordered Int32 integer bounds and includes both endpoints. `rand` keeps the legacy formula behaviour of reordering and rounding bounds inward. `chance(p)` uses percentages from 0 to 100. Round fractional results before passing them to integer-only item actions.

Dialog assignments reject undefined/nonnumeric variables, zero division, invalid arguments and non-finite results. They stop execution with source and line diagnostics before dependent actions and preserve the old variable value. Reading `${name}` numerically reads its value; it cannot inject expression syntax. Syntax inspection in the validator and palette does not consume randomness.

Stats formulas retain their legacy permissive treatment of unknown variables and division by zero (zero). Sharing syntax does not make these two error policies interchangeable.

## Extending the language

Public parameterless `IExpressionFunctionStrategy`, `IExpressionBinaryStrategy` and `IExpressionUnaryStrategy` implementations are discovered in mods that depend on `expressions_base`. Function strategies declare a name and argument bounds (`MaxArguments = -1` for unlimited); evaluate operands with `context.Value` and use `context.Random`. Operator strategies declare a symbol and precedence (1–100), with optional right associativity for binary operators. The first registration owns a name; duplicate registrations cannot override built-ins. Unloading removes strategies and registry changes invalidate parser caches.

The source-compatible `Cthangover.Mods.Stats.Formulas` APIs remain available. Implementation and examples: `mods/expressions_base/src/` and that mod's bundled guide.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/index.md: Scenarios — DSL Reference =====
`.scenario` files are the heart of the game's narrative system. Each file contains a metadata header, a `---` separator, and a script body written in a custom domain-specific language (DSL).

## File structure

```scenario
scene: target_scene_name
priority: 10
condition: QuestId.hasTag("tag_name")
save_allowed: true
is_one_run: false
light_use_time: true
---
# Script body starts after ---
background "path/to/bg"
text "- Hello, traveller." key=locale_key first=marao/normal
select "[What do you do?]" key=choice_key
option "Ask about the town" key=opt_1 -> :town
option "Leave" key=opt_2 -> :exit
:exit
switch_scene home_outside
end
```

## Metadata fields (before `---`)

| Field | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `scene` | string | *(none)* | Required for automatic scene-chain execution; a headerless script can be invoked by an interactive object or RPG event |
| `channel` | string | `dialog` | `dialog` runs in the modal scene chain; `ambient` is hosted by `npc_barks_base` and excluded from that chain |
| `priority` | int | `0` | Execution order — **lower runs earlier**. All matching scenarios are enqueued in this order |
| `after` | string | *(none)* | Scenario ID that must come before this one in the chain (ordering dependency) |
| `condition` | expression | *(none)* | Quest-condition expression. Scenario only runs if true. See `dsl/conditions/` |
| `save_allowed` | bool | `false` | Whether the player can save the game from this scene |
| `is_one_run` | bool | `false` | If true, the scenario runs only once per playthrough. After execution, it's skipped forever |
| `light_use_time` | bool | *(unset)* | If `true`, the lighting system uses the in-game time-of-day for light colors/angles; `false` forces static light colors. When the field is omitted the scene's default behavior is kept (it is parsed as a nullable bool) |

## Real examples

### Default kitchen scenario (game content)

```scenario
scene: home_kitchen
priority: 10
---
light_set [{"x":0.43,"y":0.58,"radius":500,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"},{"x":0.10,"y":0.27,"radius":500,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"}]
select "A room equipped as a kitchen" key=rooms/kitchen/prompt
option "Leave" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_exit -> :exit

:exit
switch_scene home_vestibule
end
```

### Cooking mod extension (lower priority = runs earlier)

```scenario
scene: home_kitchen
priority: 5
---
light_set [{"x":0.43,"y":0.58,"radius":500,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"}]
select "A room equipped as a kitchen" key=rooms/kitchen/prompt
option "Leave" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_exit -> :exit
option "Cook" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_cook -> :cook

:exit
switch_scene home_vestibule
end

:cook
action toggle_cooking_workbench
end
```

Because `priority: 5 < 10`, the cooking scenario is enqueued **before** the default one. Note that it does **not** replace the default — both scenarios are enqueued and run in priority order. Use `condition:` (or `is_one_run: true`) to exclude scenarios from execution, not priority.

### Bedroom with save_allowed (game content)

```scenario
scene: home_bedroom
save_allowed: true
priority: 10
---
select "Your bedroom. You can rest here." key=rooms/bedroom/prompt
option "Leave" key=rooms/bedroom/opt_exit -> :exit

:exit
switch_scene home_vestibule
end
```

### One-run event (interactives test suite)

```scenario
scene: interactives_test
priority: 10
is_one_run: true
---
interactive_add kitchen_lamp
end
```

The interactive lamp is added once, on first visit. Subsequent visits skip this scenario.

For `channel: ambient`, actor roles, spatial conditions, cooldowns and speech reactions are documented in Background NPC scenarios. These headers belong to the background host.

## Adding or replacing a scenario

A scenario is identified by its **file name** (without `.scenario`): that is the
id the completion history, `after:` links and the studio use.

- **Add** a scenario to any scene — even one from another mod — by giving it a
  name of your own (prefix it with your mod or game, e.g. `my_novel_town_shop`).
  It runs next to the scene's other scenarios, ordered by `priority`.
- **Replace** another mod's scenario by shipping a file with the **same name**
  and adding that mod to your `depends`. Mods load dependencies first, so your
  file wins; it may even target a different scene. The original mod is not
  changed.
- Two mods that ship the same name without a dependency between them have no
  defined winner — the resource atlas reports this as an error.

## How the engine selects scenarios

1. Player enters `scene: home_kitchen` (the following steps describe `channel: dialog`).
2. Engine collects all `.scenario` files targeting `home_kitchen` across all mods
3. Sorts by `priority` (ascending) and resolves `after:` dependencies (topological order)
4. For each scenario in that order, evaluates `condition:` (if present). A scenario **without** a condition always passes.
5. **Every** scenario whose condition passes is enqueued into the scene's event chain — there is no first-match cut-off. Scenarios then execute one after another in enqueue order.

Priority therefore controls *ordering*, not selection: a lower-priority number means the scenario runs earlier in the chain, alongside all other passing scenarios.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/actions/catalog.md: Action parameter catalog =====
This table follows the installed production `schemas/*.json`, including conditional integrations. Load the owning mod before using its action. Required parameters are bold; other parameters are optional. A schema is a validation/editor contract, not an implementation: the runtime action is still responsible for applying it. Test-only suites are excluded.

| Action | Owner | Parameters (schema type; default if given) |
|---|---|---|
| `battle.init` | `turn_battle_base` | **`enemies`** (ref_list:character); `scene` (scene) |
| `battle.init` | `turn_battle_base__quests` | **`enemies`** (ref_list:character); `scene` (scene); `quest_id` (ref:quest); `new_tag` (text) |
| `battle.set_core` | `turn_battle_base` | **`core`** (choice) |
| `character.add_skill` | `skills_base` | **`skill_id`** (ref:skill) |
| `character.send_notification` | `characters_base` | **`type`** (ref:character) |
| `craft.open` | `crafting` | **`station`** (ref:craft_station) |
| `equip.put` | `equipment` | **`item`** (ref:item); `who` (who); `slot` (ref:equip_slot); `give` (bool) |
| `equip.remove` | `equipment` | **`slot`** (ref:equip_slot); `who` (who) |
| `item.add` | `items_base` | **`id`** (ref:item); `count` (int; default `1`) |
| `item.remove` | `items_base` | **`id`** (ref:item); `count` (int; default `1`) |
| `map.close_zone` | `map_base` | **`zone_id`** (text) |
| `map.hide_zone` | `map_base` | **`zone_id`** (text) |
| `map.open_zone` | `map_base` | **`zone_id`** (text) |
| `map.show_zone` | `map_base` | **`zone_id`** (text) |
| `npc.look` | `npc_barks_base` | **`who`** (text); **`target`** (text) |
| `party.add` | `characters_base` | **`id`** (ref:character) |
| `quest.add_tag` | `quests_base` | **`quest_id`** (ref:quest); **`tag`** (text) |
| `quest.remove_tag` | `quests_base` | **`quest_id`** (ref:quest); **`tag`** (text) |
| `quest.send_notification` | `quests_base` | **`quest_id`** (ref:quest) |
| `quest.set_data_status` | `quests_base` | **`quest_id`** (ref:quest); **`level`** (int) |
| `quest.set_status` | `quests_base` | **`quest_id`** (ref:quest); **`status`** (choice) |
| `rpg.battle` | `rpg__battles` | **`enemies`** (ref_list:character); `win_flag` (flag) |
| `rpg.teleport` | `rpg` | `map` (ref:rpg_map); **`x`** (int); **`y`** (int); `dir` (choice) |
| `stat.add` | `stats_base` | **`stat`** (ref:stat); `value` (number; default `1`); `who` (who) |
| `stat.archetype` | `stats_base` | `id` (ref:archetype); `who` (who) |
| `stat.exp` | `stats_base` | **`value`** (int); `who` (who; default `party`) |
| `stat.restore` | `stats_base` | `who` (who; default `party`); `stat` (text) |
| `trade.open` | `trading` | **`shop`** (ref:shop) |

`add_live2d_model` is also registered by `live2d_base`; its runtime action requires `model_path=`, accepts `mod_id=`, `node_name=` (default `Live2DModel`), `x=`/`y=` together and `scale=` and it is not currently described by an action schema. `toggle_cooking_workbench` is the cooking convenience alias for its workbench panel; it has no parameters. Spatial `npc.look` requires an ambient execution host and cannot be used as a general modal action.

See Action examples, RPG, Stats, Crafting, Trading and Background scenarios for behaviour and constraints. Unknown/missing parameters and references are checked by the command palette and validator using the same schema catalog.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/actions/index.md: Scenario Actions =====
The `action` command bridges the DSL to game systems. Actions can be implemented in **C#** (via `IScenarioAction` + Roslyn compilation) or in **GDScript** (via `get_name()` + `run(ctx)` auto-detection).

## Syntax

```scenario
action namespace.action_name param1=value1 param2=value2 ...
```

Parameters use `key=value` syntax (no spaces around `=`).

## Real examples

### Character actions

```scenario
action party.add id=Murakami
```

Adds the character `Murakami` to the player's party. `id=` matches the character's `Id` field in their JSON definition.

```scenario
action character.send_notification type=Murakami
```

Triggers the "new party member" UI notification for character `Murakami`.

```scenario
action character.add_skill skill_id=slave_master
```

Grants the skill `slave_master` to the player. If the skill defines a `GrantsAction`, the action card is placed into the action pool for assignment in the character panel. Already owned skills are ignored; unknown IDs are logged and skipped.

### Battle initiation

```scenario
action battle.set_core core=card_battle
action battle.init scene=town_entry enemies=wolf_2,werewolf_girl_1 quest_id=MeetingMurakamiQuest new_tag=boss_battle
```

`battle.set_core` selects the combat ruleset before `battle.init` starts the encounter. Core IDs are **case-sensitive** and unknown IDs throw; the shipped cores register as `card_battle` and `ff_battle`. Both actions are declared by the `turn_battle_base` mod.

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| `scene` | Scene to return to after battle |
| `enemies` | Comma-separated list of enemy character IDs |
| `quest_id` | Quest to update on battle outcome |
| `new_tag` | Tag to set on the quest when the battle starts |

### UI panels

```scenario
action ui.toggle_panel name=cooking_workbench
```

`name=` is required. The panel is first looked up as a `Control` node in the scene tree by node name; if nothing matches, the name is treated as a mod UI entry id from the manifest `ui` list and toggled through the UI registry — this is how the cooking mod's workbench overlay opens. A mod can also wrap the call into its own action: the cooking mod registers `toggle_cooking_workbench` for exactly that.

The action parameter catalog lists every production action schema, including items, stats, equipment, crafting, trading, RPG and ambient speech. The tables below explain the common actions in more detail.

## Available action namespaces

### `character.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `party.add` | `id=CharId` | Add character to player's party (mod `characters_base`) |
| `character.send_notification` | `type=CharId` | Show "new member" notification |
| `character.add_skill` | `skill_id=SkillId` | Grant a skill and its action card |

### `battle.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `battle.init` | `scene`, `enemies`, `quest_id`, `new_tag` | Start a battle |
| `battle.set_core` | `core=card_battle` | Select combat ruleset by its case-sensitive core ID |

### `quest.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `quest.set_status` | `quest_id=Q status=Progress` | Set the quest lifecycle state by `QuestStatus` enum name: `NotStarted`, `Progress`, or `End` |
| `quest.set_data_status` | `quest_id=Q level=3` | Set the quest numeric progress counter |
| `quest.add_tag` | `quest_id=Q tag=T` | Add a tag to a quest |
| `quest.remove_tag` | `quest_id=Q tag=T` | Remove a tag |
| `quest.send_notification` | `quest_id=Q` | Trigger "Quest Updated" UI popup |

> **Note:** The `status` parameter is parsed as a case-insensitive `QuestStatus` **enum name** (`NotStarted` / `Progress` / `End`) via `Enums<QuestStatus>.Parse`. Use enum names in scenarios. Numeric strings such as `status=2` only happen to work because `Enum.Parse` aliases numbers to enum values — do not rely on this. For numeric progress use `quest.set_data_status` with `level=N` instead.

### `scene.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `scene.instantiate` | `path=res://scenes/ui/CookingPanel.tscn name=CookingNode` | Load and add a PackedScene to the scene tree |
| `scene.remove_object` | `name=CookingNode` | Remove a named node from the scene tree |

### `ui.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `ui.toggle_panel` | `name=PanelName` | Toggle a `Control` node by scene name, or a manifest `ui` entry by id |

### `lighting.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `lighting.clear_map` | *(none)* | Reset lighting depth/albedo maps |

### `map.*`

World-map zone control. Every action takes the required `zone_id=ZoneId` parameter; a missing value is logged and the action does nothing. Overrides are stored by the map service and persisted in the save.

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `map.open_zone` | `zone_id=Z` | Open a zone for travel (counterpart to `"locked": true` in the map JSON) |
| `map.close_zone` | `zone_id=Z` | Close a zone — the marker stays but travel is blocked |
| `map.show_zone` | `zone_id=Z` | Reveal a hidden zone on the map |
| `map.hide_zone` | `zone_id=Z` | Remove a zone from the map entirely |

## Adding a custom action

Define an action class in your mod's `src/`:

```csharp
public class OpenTreasureChestAction : IScenarioAction
{
    public string Name => "treasure.open";

    public void Run(IActionContext ctx)
    {
        var item = ctx.GetParam("item");
        var count = ctx.GetParam("count");
        ctx.Log("SCENARIO", $"Gave player {count}x {item}");
    }
}
```

`IActionContext` exposes `GetParam(string)` for reading action parameters plus the game subsystem services and `Log`/`LogWarning` methods (see `Core/Actions/IActionContext.cs`). There is no typed getter such as `GetParamInt` — parse values yourself.

### Registration

You do **not** need to register actions manually: every implementation of `IScenarioAction` in your mod's compiled assembly is discovered automatically by reflection when the assembly is loaded.

If you need to register an assembly manually (outside the standard mod loading flow), call:

```csharp
ScenarioActionFactory.Instance.RegisterAssembly(assembly);
```

If two actions register the same `Name`, the earliest registration wins and later duplicates are silently ignored.

Then use in scenarios:

```scenario
action treasure.open item=food/ration count=5
```

### From GDScript (no Roslyn needed)

Create `scripts/my_actions.gd`:

```gdscript
extends RefCounted

func get_name():
    return "my.quest_activate"

func run(ctx):
    var questId = ctx.GetParam("quest_id")
    ctx.Quests.SetStatus(questId, "Progress")
    ctx.Quests.SendNotification(questId)
    ctx.Log("EVENT", "Quest " + questId + " activated via GDScript")
```

Declare in `manifest.json`:

```jsonc
{
  "name": "My GDScript Mod",                               // Human-readable mod name
  "gd_sources": ["scripts/*.gd"]                           // Glob patterns for .gd files — auto-discovered for actions, conditions, etc.
}
```

The kernel auto-discovers `get_name()` + `run()` and registers the action — no factory or provider code needed. The `ctx` parameter provides access to all game subsystems (Quests, Character, Battle, Inventory, etc.). See GDScript in Mods for the full action context API.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/dsl/index.md: DSL Command Reference =====
Dialog strategies provide the standard commands below; `select`/`option` are handled specially by the parser. Dialog assignments `set`/`cset` use the shared `expressions_base` evaluator, and optional mechanics add commands such as `check`. The active command set is determined by the loaded mods, so no fixed total describes every game.

---

## Flow Control

### `select`
Opens a choice menu. String is the prompt text (can be a locale key or literal).

```scenario
select "What now?" key=choice/prompt
option "Go home" key=choice/home -> :home
option "Explore" key=choice/explore -> :explore
```

- Must be followed by one or more `option` commands
- The `key=` parameter references a locale key for localization
- If no `key=`, the text itself is displayed
- Supports the same `first=`/`second=` avatar parameters as `text`

A `select` with no following `option` commands is silently dropped from the queue — no error is raised.

### `option`
Defines a choice within a `select` block. Three ways to specify the jump target:

```scenario
# Arrow form
option "Leave" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_exit -> :exit

# Named parameter form
option "Leave" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_exit goto=exit

# Bare positional label form
option "Leave" :exit
```

- Text can be a literal or a `key=` locale reference
- All three target forms are equivalent; the `->` arrow takes priority when several are present
- An `option` outside a `select` block is ignored
- `if=` hides the answer while its condition is false; quote the whole argument when it contains spaces: `option "Pay" "if=item('money/coin') >= 5" -> :pay`

### `:label`
Anchor point for jumps. Must start with `:` at the beginning of a line.

```scenario
:enter_room
background "town/meetingmurakami/00079"
text "[Marao stepped inside]" key=enter/room
```

Labels are local to the scenario file. They cannot be jumped to from other scenarios.

### `goto`
Unconditional jump to a label.

```scenario
goto -> :cleanup
```

or equivalently:

```scenario
goto target=cleanup
```

> **Note:** The bare form `goto :cleanup` is **not** supported — it is silently ignored by the parser. Always use the `->` arrow or the `target=` parameter.

Useful for skipping optional content or implementing complex branching without nested options.

### `when`

Evaluate the shared condition language at the current line:

```scenario
when "door_open == 1" else=locked -> :enter
```

Without a matching branch target execution continues. Add `wait=true timeout=8` to re-evaluate until the condition becomes true or a positive finite deadline expires; success uses the arrow, timeout uses `else`. See Background scenarios for a complete waiting example and spatial conditions.

### `end`
Terminates the scenario script. Required at the end of every execution path.

```scenario
end
```

---

## Scene & Visuals

### `background`
Sets or changes the background image.

```scenario
background "town/meetingmurakami/00002-1920617752"
```

Path is relative to the mod's `backgrounds/` folder. No `.png` extension.

### `background_color`
Sets a solid color background (overrides image).

```scenario
background_color "#1a1a2e"
```

Accepts an HTML hex color (`#1a1a2e`) or a named color (`black`, `white`, `red`, `green`, `blue`, `yellow`, `gray`/`grey`). Anything else falls back to black. Useful for transitions, flashbacks, or UI overlays.

### `background_show_hide`
Animates the background layer in or out with a fade.

```scenario
background_show_hide hide
background_show_hide show duration=0.5 wait=true
```

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| (positional) | `show` or `hide` (defaults to `show`; any other value is treated as `hide`) |
| `duration=` | Fade duration in seconds (default `1`) |
| `wait=` | `wait=true` pauses the script until the fade animation completes |

### `switch_scene`
Transitions to a different scene.

```scenario
switch_scene home_vestibule
switch_scene Battle
```

The target is a scene name defined in `scenes/{name}.json`.

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| (positional) | Scene name to switch to |
| `speed=` | Transition speed as a float (default `4`) |
| `hidden=` | `hidden=true` transitions showing only the scene background |

The names `mainmenu` and `menu` are built-in: they are routed to the engine's native Godot scenes instead of mod-defined scenes. Any other name may be a scenario scene or a scene route registered by a mod through `SceneRouteRegistry` — for example `battle` is registered by `turn_battle_base` (fade transition plus combat music), so the core does not hardcode it.

### `foreground`
Sets or clears the foreground layer image (rendered above characters).

```scenario
foreground "effects/rain"
foreground ""
```

### `effect`
Plays a visual effect by ID. The syntax is `effect <effect_id>`:

```scenario
effect rain_overlay
```

The ID references a Godot scene (`.tscn`/`.scn`) from the mod's `effects/` resource group, resolved via the effect factory and instantiated above the scene. The script continues immediately — the effect plays independently and persists until its own lifetime ends.

> **Note:** Effect scenes are only loadable from **folder mods** — zip mods skip them with a warning, because `PackedScene` cannot be reconstructed from raw bytes in managed code.

---

## Text & Dialogue

### `text`
Displays a dialogue line with optional avatar portraits.

```scenario
text "- Dear lady!" key=meeting/greeting first=marao/think second=murakami/fear_1
```

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| `key=` | Locale key for the text content |
| `first=` | Left-side avatar: `{character}/{expression}` |
| `second=` | Right-side avatar |
| `hide_color=` | `hide_color=true` shows avatars in shader-based silhouette mode (color suppressed) — useful for flashbacks or unknown speakers |
| `wait=` | `wait=false` advances the script immediately without waiting for a click |
| `who=`, `duration=`, `cue=` | Background host speaker role, positive finite speech duration in seconds, and speech-event cue; see Background scenarios |
| (inline) | If no `key=`, the text is displayed literally |

### `ptext`
Like `text`, but enables runtime `${var}` variable substitution in the text content.

```scenario
set player_name Marao
ptext "Well met, ${player_name}!"
```

The dialogue box is still shown, and `first=`/`second=` avatar parameters work the same as in `text`. Use `ptext` whenever you need to interpolate variables into a line; plain `text` does not substitute variables. Variables that are not set resolve to `?`.

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| `key=` | Locale key for the text content (substitution still applies after lookup) |
| `first=` | Left-side avatar: `{character}/{expression}` |
| `second=` | Right-side avatar |
| `hide_color=` | `hide_color=true` shows avatars in shader-based silhouette mode |
| `wait=` | `wait=false` advances the script immediately without waiting for a click |
| (inline) | If no `key=`, the text is displayed literally (with `${var}` substitution) |

### `title`
Displays a large centered title, often used for chapter/section headers.

```scenario
title "Chapter 1 — The Hangover"
```

The text can be a literal or a `key=` locale reference; lines without resolvable text are silently skipped.

---

## Audio

### `music`
Switches the background music playlist.

```scenario
music playlists/tavern
music playlists/battle
```

Target is a playlist file in `music/playlists/`.

### `music_play`
Resumes paused music.

```scenario
music_play
```

### `music_pause`
Pauses the current music track.

```scenario
music_pause
```

### `sound`
Plays a one-shot sound effect.

```scenario
sound ui/click
sound battle/hit
```

Path is relative to the mod's `sounds/` folder. No `.ogg` extension.

---

## Lighting

### `light_set`
Configures dynamic 2D light sources. Accepts a JSON array of light objects.

```scenario
light_set [{"x":0.43,"y":0.58,"radius":500,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"}]
```

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `x`, `y` | Normalized position (0.0–1.0, relative to the viewport) |
| `radius` | Light radius in pixels |
| `influence` | Intensity multiplier (0.0–1.0) |
| `color` | Hex color (`#ffff00`) |

Multiple lights in one array:

```scenario
light_set [
  {"x":0.84,"y":0.04,"radius":600,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"},
  {"x":0.29,"y":0.16,"radius":600,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"}
]
```

At most 10 static lights are applied (plus the player's lamp); extra array entries are ignored.

Call with no arguments to reset all lights:

```scenario
light_set
```

### `light_use_time`
Enables/disables time-of-day influence on light color and angle.

```scenario
light_use_time true
light_use_time false
```

When enabled, the shader system adjusts warm/cool tones based on in-game time.

---

### `delay`
Pauses script execution for N seconds.

```scenario
delay 2.5
text "..."
delay 1.0
text "Oh."
```

Extended forms:

```scenario
# Invisible pause — no dialogue box activity
delay 1.5 hidden

# Pause with a caption shown during the wait
delay 3.0 "Meanwhile, across town..."

# Same, but the caption is resolved from a locale key
delay 3.0 "..." key=scene/meanwhile_caption
```

- The duration defaults to `1` second if omitted
- `hidden` can also be given as a named parameter: `delay 1.5 hidden=true`
- `key=` only applies when a text to display was given

### `animation`
Plays a sprite animation. Supports two forms:

Single sprite base with frame controls:

```scenario
animation marao/walk start=0 count=6 speed=8.0 loop=true
```

Multiple explicit sprites:

```scenario
animation sprites=town/rain_1,town/rain_2,town/rain_3 speed=4.0 loop=true
```

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| (positional) | Sprite base path (first form only) |
| `sprites=` | Comma-separated sprite list (alternative to positional base) |
| `start=` | First frame index (default `0`, first form only) |
| `count=` | Number of frames to play (default `1`) |
| `speed=` | Playback speed as a float multiplier (default `1.0`) |
| `loop=` | `true`/`false` — loop the animation (defaults to `true` when omitted) |

There is no `duration=` parameter — control timing via `speed=` and `count=`.

---

## Interactive Objects

### `interactive_add`
Adds a clickable interactive object to the scene.

```scenario
interactive_add kitchen_lamp
```

The name references an interactive definition in `interactives/{name}.json`.

### `interactive_remove`
Removes a specific interactive object.

```scenario
interactive_remove kitchen_lamp
```

### `interactive_clear`
Removes all interactive objects from the current scene.

```scenario
interactive_clear
```

### `interactive_set`
Enables, disables, shows, or hides a specific interactive object.

```scenario
interactive_set kitchen_lamp enabled=false
interactive_set cabinet_door visible=true
```

Supported named parameters: `enabled=true|false`, `visible=true|false`.

---

## Dialog Visibility

### `show_dialog`
Shows the dialogue box UI (if hidden).

```scenario
show_dialog
```

### `hide_dialog`
Hides the dialogue box UI without clearing text.

```scenario
hide_dialog
```

Used for cutscenes where you want the background visible without UI.

---

## Variables & Logic

### `set`
Stores a variable in the dialog runtime's variable store for later use — readable via `${var}` substitution in `ptext` lines and via `ctx.GetParam(...)` in actions.

```scenario
set met_murakami true
set door_locked false
```

The syntax is **space-separated**: first token is the name, second token is the value. Do not write `set name = value` with spaced equals — the parser stores the literal `"="` as the value. The no-space form `set name=expression` is a numeric assignment supplied by `expressions_base`. `cset name expression` and `cset name=expression` also calculate; see Expressions for functions, validation and extension strategies.

`set` variables live only while this dialog runs. For state that scenario conditions read, use `flag`.

### `cset`

Calculate one dialog variable using the shared expression engine:

```scenario
set reward=rnd(3,5)
cset total "1+2+${reward}"
ptext "Total: ${total}"
```

Unlike literal `set name value`, this rejects nonnumeric variables, division by zero and non-finite results before later actions execute.

### `flag`
Sets a **story flag** of the game. Unlike `set`, a flag is game state: it is saved, a new game starts
without it, and scenario `condition:` headers read it. Setting a flag rescans the scene's events, so a
scenario waiting for it can start as soon as this dialog ends.

```scenario
flag door open
flag visits 3
```

```scenario
scene: home_kitchen
condition: door == "open" && visits >= 3
---
text "The door stands open now."
```

Use the space-separated literal form. In an object or map event, `flag self.done 1` addresses only its own scope (`<map>/<local_id>:done`); a global flag uses a plain name. Numeric values compare as integers in conditions.

### `empty`
A no-op command — it queues a placeholder action that does nothing and is skipped instantly.

```scenario
empty
```

Labels (`:label`) are implemented as named `empty` markers, so a bare `empty` line is also a convenient placeholder slot during development.

---

## Notifications

### `notify`
Queues a notification banner in the top-left corner of the screen. Notifications appear one by one, stack vertically, and auto-dismiss after 10 seconds. Use for quest updates, item pickups, or any non-blocking informational messages.

```scenario
notify "Quest updated: Find the key"
```

```scenario
notify "Received: Health Potion"
```

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| (inline) | The notification text to display |
| `key=` | Locale key for localization (takes priority over inline text) |

Notifications are queued in a buffer — they appear in order and will wait if the screen already has the maximum number of visible notifications (4). The 10-second timer starts only when the notification becomes visible, not when it is queued.

---

## Special Syntax

### `#` — Comments

```scenario
# This line is ignored by the parser
# Use comments to document complex branching
```

### `---` — Metadata Separator

Separates the YAML-like metadata header from the script body. Must appear on its own line.

### `->` — Jump Operator

Used in `option` and `goto` commands to point to `:label` targets.

```scenario
option "Go inside" -> :enter
```

### `${path}` — Include Directive

Inside `.scenario` scripts and other mod text files, `${ path/to/file }` is replaced with the full content of the referenced file at **mod resolution time** — before the script is ever parsed. This happens while the mod loader reads the file (the same mechanism used by `ScenarioResolver` and the other text-based resolvers).

```scenario
# dialogue/intro.scenario
${ events/common_ambience.txt }
background "town/meetingmurakami/00079"
```

- Includes are resolved relative to the mod root and may be nested (an included file can contain includes of its own)
- If the path does not resolve and `path + ".json"` exists, that file is used instead
- Circular includes are detected and reported as errors; resolution fails for the affected file
- Whitespace inside the braces is allowed (`${ path }`)

Do not confuse this with the runtime `${var}` substitution in dialogue text (see `ptext`): includes are expanded once when the mod loads, variables are substituted every time the line is shown.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/dsl/conditions/index.md: Shared scenario conditions =====
The `condition:` metadata field uses a simple expression language to control which scenarios execute based on quest state and scene flags. Boolean literals `true` and `false` are also valid condition expressions.

## Syntax

```scenario
condition: QuestId.method("arg") [&& || ! expr ...]
```

## Available methods

| Method | Description |
|---|---|
| `hasTag("tag")` | True if the quest has the specified tag |
| `notHasTag("tag")` | True if the quest does NOT have the tag |
| `IsActive` | True when quest Status equals `Progress` |
| `Status <op> N` | Quest status compared against number `N` |
| `DataStatus <op> N` | Quest numeric counter compared against number `N` |

Supported comparison operators (for both quest `Status`/`DataStatus` and flag comparisons): `==`, `!=`, `>`, `>=`, `<`, `<=`.

## Custom GDScript conditions

Mods can add custom condition names via GDScript. A GDScript with `get_condition_name()` + `evaluate(flags, args)` is auto-registered into the condition parser.

`scripts/weather_conditions.gd`:
```gdscript
extends RefCounted

func get_condition_name():
    return "gds.is_rain"

func evaluate(flags, args):
    return flags.get("weather", "") == "rain"
```

Usage in scenario:
```scenario
condition: gds.is_rain
```

With arguments:
```gdscript
func get_condition_name():
    return "gds.flag_equals"

func evaluate(flags, args):
    var expected = args.get("0", "")
    return flags.get("custom_flag", "") == expected
```

```scenario
condition: gds.flag_equals("victory")
```
